河流固有冲刷携运之功。但它们席卷而下的物质，终究丢失的并不多。更多的替我们保存了下来：面对海洋，是以陆源带（terrigene bands）的形式；面对内陆海，是以三角洲的形式——人在其上昌盛无比，在那里筑起自己最辉煌的城。看那三角洲状的地图：尼罗河巨大的膝头向北顶进，把地中海挤向一旁；恒河与布拉马普特拉河（Brahmaputra）共用一个河口，那河口的长牙年甚一年，向南啃进孟加拉湾；半个路易斯安那不过是密西西比河的一条尖舌，舐吞着墨西哥湾，草原的泥浆与红树沼泽赛跑，要给迪克西（Dixie）再添一个凸出的佛罗里达。
一切湖泊都在皱缩。欧洲的湖泊——那些「前湖」——半数已化为冲积旱地。另一半正步其后尘。把今日的日内瓦湖与罗马时代的莱芒湖（Leman）比较一下；距游人如织的湖滨远远深入内陆之处，古老帝国的诸港正搁浅在那里。
一切其余的陆上营力，手挽着手向水作战。
大气营力。空气本身正缓缓漏逸到外层空间去，空气中的水分也随之而去。
喷发营力。在行将到来、火山活动更烈的时期，各地或许会死掉几十万人，但人类整体将赢得喘息；火山口将喷吐出熔岩的帝国，增添我们的陆地储备。单单一场上乘的喷发，从大地腹中抛出的陆地便足以抵偿数千年的侵蚀。

===Original===

Animal ones. Mightier than earthworm for the water’s side, fight polyp and coral for the waterless. After a submarine eruption, the coral insects at once set to and build around the new-formed crater. One pink generation dies; the next one begins on the skeletons, pitilessly, as calcareous platform for its own campaign. Land peeps out. The mariner sees before him a new atoll not seen on his chart, a truly magical new island of desire. Ocean is that much the smaller.

Squarest denial is reserved for the two prime aqueous arguments.

First, that the sea has in point of evident fact gained on the land.

The land has in point of evident fact gained on the sea. Those terrestrial forests and their fossils discovered under the ocean, proving that where now is sea was land, are demonstrably less spacious than those marine deposits discovered far inland and high up, miles from the shore and thousands of feet above it; than those older sedimentary rocks, once deposited in the deeps, which now tower on the loftiest Andean cones or in highest Himalaya﻿—proving that where now is land was sea.

===Chinese===

动物营力。蚯蚓为水方效力，而珊瑚虫与珊瑚为无水的一方作战，且更为强猛。海底喷发之后，珊瑚虫立即动手，围绕新生的火山口筑造。粉红的一代死去；下一代便毫不留情地在那些骸骨上开工，充作自己战役的钙质平台。陆地探出头来。水手看见面前一座海图上未载的新环礁，一座真正神奇、令人渴慕的新岛。海洋便又小了这一分。
最直截了当的否认，专留给水的两大首要论点。
其一，说：就明显的事实而言，海已经侵占了陆地。
就明显的事实而言，陆地已经侵占了海。海底发现的那些陆地森林及其化石，证明今日为海之处曾是陆地，但它们显然不如那些在深入内陆、高高在上、离岸数英里、高出海面数千英尺之处发现的海洋沉积物来得广阔；也不如那些曾沉积于深水中的更古老的沉积岩——它们如今高耸在最巍峨的安第斯群锥之上、最高的喜马拉雅山中——证明今日为陆之处曾是海洋。

===Original===

I’ve seen what was most solid earth before
					

					Become a Sea, the Sea become a Shore;
					

					Far from the Sea Sea-Cocles often lie
					

					And Anchors old are found on Mountains high;
					

					Land-floods have made a Valley of a Plain
					

					And brought a Mountain with them to the Main.

===Chinese===

	我见过原先最坚实的泥土
	变成海，海又变成岸；
	离海远处常躺着海的蚶子，
	古老的船锚在高山上寻见；
	山洪把平原冲成一道谷，
	又卷着一座山同赴大海。

===Original===

Impartial Ovid! Impartial oceanography allows that both *Metamorphoses* occur; but the sea-receding one more largely. According to the preachers of the Six Cycles﻿—the periodical up-and-down movement of the ocean floor, first an age of tilting up and mountain-building, then an alternate age of transgressional seas and continent-sinking﻿—in the end it is the land that wins. The waters have shifted, but they have lost. The vast bulk of the Ganges delta and Gangetic plains, if it shows how vastly the Himalayas must have been lowered to build them, shows chiefly that what the heights were denuded of has not been lost for terra firma. The cyclic seas once swallowed up Gondwanaland, the old great Indian Atlantis; the aquophobe cycle replied by building up the new great peninsula of Hindustan.

Second, that there is now more water than land.

There is more land than water. It is only the trifling mass of the sea-depths that is greater than the trifling mass of the raised earth; below the deepest Pacific soundings, as below all the continents, there is solid earth right down unto the fires within. Taking the total cubic capacity of the globe, solid outnumbers liquid a multitude of times. As the earth ages and shrinks, this disproportion will augment, slow desiccation continuing till the last drop of water is dried up, till the world is a desert of dead men.

===Chinese===

不偏不倚的奥维德（Ovid）！不偏不倚的海洋学也承认：两种《变形记》（*Metamorphoses*）都在发生；只是海退的一种规模更大。照「六循环」（the Six Cycles）的信徒们说——洋底周期性的升降运动：先是一个翘升造山的时代，随后交替而来一个海侵与陆沉的时代——到头来获胜的是陆地。诸水迁移过了，可它们输了。恒河三角洲与恒河平原那庞大的体量，即使显出喜马拉雅山必曾被何等大幅地削低才筑成了它们，主要更显出：高处被剥蚀之物并未脱离坚实大地（terra firma）而亡失。循环之海一度吞没过冈瓦纳大陆（Gondwanaland），那古老伟大的印度亚特兰蒂斯（Atlantis）；怕水的循环则回敬以筑起新的伟大半岛印度斯坦。
其二，说：如今水多于陆。
陆多于水。唯有深海那一点点质量，才比隆起陆地那一点点质量为大；在太平洋最深测点之下，正如在一切大陆之下，向下直到内部的烈火，全是坚实的土地。取这颗行星的总容积而论，固体超出液体许多倍。随着地球衰老、收缩，这一失衡还将加剧，缓慢的干化继续下去，直到最后一滴水烤干，直到世界成为一片死人的荒漠。

===Original===

Consider also the heavens. The moon, being smaller than the earth, dried and died sooner, and now is a cloudless, sealess, waterless world, image of what we one day shall be. Venus, our younger sister, still modestly veiled in a garment of white clouds, is an image of our fresh innocence in the past, a world of young sparkling waters. Mars, our elder brother, has few clouds left to cover him; he is parching rapidly; there roll no oceans more for his warlike ships to ride on, only inland black seas, paltry Euxines. Along his two belts the thirsty deserts are spreading. They are spreading along ours. Along Cancer. Central Asia is perishing, Gobi grows; Babylon and Nineveh are under the sand, Arabia Petraea waxes. In the Holiest Land not all the gold of Zionism will make the milk and honey flow again; the waves of the Sahara now greet the waves of the sea and have long engulfed the richest granary of Rome, devouring Rome; despite Wall Street the Great American Desert remains one. Along Capricorn. The South Australian and South African and South American wastes are not receding, any more than the vaster opaline wastes on Mars. Who still has vegetation in his dried sea-bottoms, as here the last herb will sprout feebly from the parched Pacific floor.

Being made up of seven parts water in ten, from a water-losing much sooner than from a land-losing planet man is doomed to go. Before the globe was cool enough for water to appear, organic life did not exist; carbon could not combine to form the plasm. It will not exist when the globe is too cold for water to remain. An Ark for the Deluge; there’s no Ark against the Drought.

Without water, vegetation must die, and the herbiverous creatures that live on it, and we that live on them both. Surely the people is grass.

===Chinese===

也抬头看看诸天。月亮比地球小，干得早、死得早，如今是一个无云、无海、无水的世界，是我们有朝一日的写照。金星是我们的幼妹，仍谦逊地蒙着一袭白云的衣裳，是我们往昔新鲜纯真的写照，一个青春荡漾、波光潋滟的水世界。火星是我们的兄长，遮体的云已所剩无几；他正迅速焦干；再没有海洋供他好战的舰船驰骋，只剩内陆的黑色海洋，区区几个攸克辛（Euxine）。沿着他的两条腰带，渴旱的沙漠正在蔓延。它们也正沿着我们的腰带蔓延。沿着巨蟹带（Cancer）。中亚在死去，戈壁在生长；巴比伦与尼尼微没于沙下，石质阿拉伯（Arabia Petraea）在扩张。在那至圣之地，犹太复国主义（Zionism）的全部黄金也换不回奶与蜜重新流淌；撒哈拉的沙浪如今已与海浪相迎，早已吞没罗马最富饶的谷仓，正在吞噬罗马；任凭华尔街如何，大美利坚沙漠依旧是大美利坚沙漠。沿着摩羯带（Capricorn）。澳大利亚南部、非洲南部与南美洲的荒芜并无退缩，正如火星上那更辽阔的蛋白石色荒芜也并无退缩。这位兄长干涸的海底至今还有植被，正如在这里，最后一茎草也将从焦渴的太平洋底孱弱地抽芽。
人身既十分之七是水，那么注定要亡于一颗失水的行星，而远不会先亡于一颗失陆的行星。地球尚未冷却到足以让水出现之前，有机生命并不存在；碳无法化合成原生质（plasm）。待到地球冷得留不住水，生命也将不复存在。洪水有方舟；旱灾却没有方舟。
没有水，草木必死，以草木为生的食草生灵必死，以这两者为生的我们也必死。「这百姓诚然是草。」

---

			

===Original===

Flood certain; drought certain﻿—how far is there paradox? Thus far: that, save the Living Terror, nothing is certain.

Yet, though no certitude, both the dry-land school and the blue-water school have some chance of being justified; in the long up-and-down future of geological time one individual factor, on the one side or the other, may rise suddenly eminent above all probable calculation, and, its allies gathering insolence with it, and all of these acquiring momentum together, may be able to tilt the balance for its own side and win to time, without yet proving the opposite prophets, who also tabled on averages and likelihoods, either knaves or fools. Is it paradox to say that the average man has about equal chances of dying of thirst in Arabia or of drowning in the Atlantic, of succumbing to dropsy or disease of dryness?

Equal chances, equally remote; postponement rather than paradox. For these two hold pace with each other, cancel each other, defeat each other, in terraqueous equilibrium delay each other, until that far day when the earth, having eluded all accidents and pulled through all illnesses, will perish only, when her hour is up, of old age or its other name, the Cold.

===Chinese===

洪水属必然；干旱属必然——悖论究竟深到几许？深到这一步：除了活的恐怖（the Living Terror），没有什么确定无疑。
然而，虽无确定性，旱陆派与碧水派却都各有机会言中；在地质时代漫长起伏的将来，某一个单独的因素，或在彼方或在此方，可能骤然显赫，凌驾于一切或然的计算之上，它的盟友们随之气焰日盛，全体一起积得冲势，或许便能为自己一方压倒天平、赢得时间，而同时却不必证明对面的先知们——他们同样押在平均数与或然率上——是骗子或是傻子。说常人在阿拉伯渴死与大西洋溺死的机会约略相等、罹水肿而亡与罹干耗而亡的机会约略相等，这算悖论么？
机会相等，同样遥远；与其说是悖论，不如说是展期。因为这两者并驾齐驱，互相抵消，互相挫败，在水陆的均衡中互相拖延，直到那遥远的一天——地球既已躲过一切意外、熬过一切疾病，届时时辰一尽，便将只死于衰老，或衰老的别名——寒冷（the Cold）。

### Cold / 寒冷

===Original===

Not accident nor illness, mishap nor malady, not warmth nor water nor desire of water; but the Cold. Is this, at last, the doom inevitable; the patient doom that can outwait the others, that though all they should fail us will not fail?

The man of science, who cancels flood by drought and drought by flood, laughs at all fiery threatenings and puts comet-fears with fairytales, now laughs no longer. Even he knows the world must die, and this is his chosen death. He plumps for the cold and the darkness.

Nor is he here in conflict with older prophets: some Hebrew ones﻿—the day of the Lord, cried Amos, it is darkness and not light﻿—and Norse ones, who foretold that Balder god of brightness must die in the end, when Fimbul-Winter will return again and forever. Nor in conflict with the heart’s own last vision of terror﻿—Eternity: the Cold and the Darkness.

===Chinese===

不是意外也不是疾病，不是横祸也不是沉疴，不是暖热、不是水、也不是对水的渴求；而是寒冷（the Cold）。这，终究是否就是那无可逃避的末日；那有耐心的末日，能熬过其他一切——纵使它们全都负我们而去，它也决不失约？
那位以旱抵消洪、以洪抵消旱的科学中人，对一切烈火的恫吓一笑置之，把对彗星的畏惧归入童话，如今却笑不起来了。连他也知道世界必死，而这便是他选定的死法。他一口咬定：寒冷与黑暗。
在这点上，他与更古老的先知们也没有冲突：有些希伯来先知——阿摩司（Amos）呼喊过，耶和华的日子，是黑暗没有光明——还有诺斯先知，他们预言光明之神巴德尔（Balder）终必死去，那时芬布尔之冬（Fimbul-Winter）将再度降临，永驻不逝。也不与人心自己那最终的恐怖异象相冲突——永恒：寒冷与黑暗。

---

			

===Original===

On this hypothesis, as on others, there may be cooperation for destruction. Cold may be bound up with or incidental to or helped out by some other agency.

Many, perhaps most, adherents of the No Water school, think we shall hardly get so far as to fail of direct drought. Water, which absorbs, retains and gives out again the heat the sun sendeth, in slowly disappearing from the atmosphere is rendering it slowly colder. No oxygen will save, when that fractional drop of water becomes more fractional; the world will become too cold to live in, as a result of its dryness, before it becomes too dry.

Our vanishing air﻿—not only the vanishing water in it﻿—secretly collaborates. It is going the way of the moon’s lost atmosphere; with it will go our best protection from the cold; without it we shall succumb. It will lessen as the sun’s heat lessens, just when we need it most.

The terrestrial movements contribute. Whereby we may die of cold in two separate stages: one hemisphere of earlier palsy, the other of final frost. Earth’s two different forms of rotation proceeding at different speeds, partition strains result; the strains become tides; the tides act as brakes. Forspent, the world slows down; halts, wearily creeps to paralysis. At last the two rotations will coincide. In that hour, there will be no seasons more: one hemisphere will be turned forever to the night; the other, warmer awhile, all the year and for all the years to the sun﻿—till he too, the prime destroyer, shall go out and, all other deaths avoided, achieve man’s total end.

===Chinese===

在这一假说上，正如在其他假说上一样，毁灭也可能有协同合作。寒冷也许与另一种营力相纠结，或附随于它，或得它相助。
「无水」学派的许多信众——也许是大多数——认为我们多半走不到直接旱亡的那一步。水吸收、保存、再放出太阳送来的热；水从大气中缓缓消失，也正使大气缓缓变冷。当那本已只占零头的水分愈发成为更小的零头时，氧气救不了我们；世界将因其干燥而冷得无法居住，还等不到它先干渴到那一步。
我们正在消散的空气——不仅是其中正在消散的水分——也在暗中协作。它正走上月亮那层失去的大气的老路；随之而去的将是我们抵御寒冷的最好屏障；没有了它，我们必将倒下。它将随着太阳热力的衰减而衰减，恰恰在我们最需要它的时候。
地球的运动也来出力。由此我们可能分两个阶段死于寒冷：一个半球较早瘫痪，另一个半球最终霜封。地球两种不同形式的旋转以不同的速度进行，遂产生分区的应变；应变化为潮汐；潮汐充作刹车。力气耗尽，世界慢下来；停住，疲惫地爬向瘫痪。最后两种旋转终将重合。到那时辰，四季不复存在：一个半球将永远朝向黑夜；另一个半球温暖一时，全年、终年朝着太阳——直到他，那首要的毁灭者，也熄灭，在避开了一切其他死亡之后，完成人类的彻底终结。

===Original===

What is the Sun?

Hor, Sol, Ra; Sūrya Mitra, Savitr; Helios, Phaethon, Phoebus Apollo﻿—he has had many names; many temples from Persia to Mexico, from Stonehenge to Xauxa, from the high Quirinal in Rome, golden Rome﻿—*Urbs solaris imperialis*﻿—to golden Cuzco, gold within, gold without, *Cosa Sagrada*, *la imperial ciudad*. In every mythology he has been a deity, or at the lowest a mighty man﻿—do the Andaman Islanders confirm their title as the lowest of peoples by accounting him a woman, and the wife of the moon at that? He has been worshipped since there were worshippers, adored since souls knew adoration. He is a god.

He is a star. A middle-aged star of middle size and, as social brilliance in the heavens is reckoned, of strictly middle-class station: no Betelgeuse for bulk, no Sirius for glory, Companion of Sirius for dwarf intensity of glory. His burning heart is enclosed within the photosphere, a layer of red-hot ocean of gas, deeper than Asia, lashed all around into waves and Everest-high jets of scarlet spray that from the earth would sprinkle the moon; the chromosphere, his cloak of helium and hydrogen; the corona of coronium; the light zodiacal. His heat in the centre runs to millions, at the surface to thousands of degrees. Better guide than the mercury is to feel him shining in his strength on a Sahara noon, an Aden teatime, and to reflect that he is sending us a two thousand millionth part of it, and sending it from over ninety million miles away.

===Chinese===

太阳是什么？
霍尔（Hor）、索尔（Sol）、拉（Ra）；苏利耶（Sūrya）、密多罗（Mitra）、萨维陀利（Savitr）；赫利俄斯（Helios）、法厄同（Phaethon）、福玻斯·阿波罗（Phoebus Apollo）——他有过许多名字；有过许多神庙，从波斯到墨西哥，从巨石阵（Stonehenge）到绍沙（Xauxa），从罗马高处的奎里纳勒山（Quirinal）——金色的罗马，太阳帝国的都城（*Urbs solaris imperialis*）——到金色的库斯科（Cuzco），内里是金，外表是金，圣物（*Cosa Sagrada*），帝王之都（*la imperial ciudad*）。在一切神话里他都曾是神，至低也曾是一位伟人——安达曼群岛人（Andaman Islanders）把他算作一个女人、而且还是月亮之妻，他们是否以此坐实了自己最低等民族的名号？自有敬拜者以来，他就一直被敬拜；自灵魂懂得崇敬以来，他就一直被崇敬。他是一位神。
他是一颗恒星。一颗中年的恒星，中等的大小，按诸天社会里的辉煌而论，严格属于中产阶级的地位：论块头，算不得参宿四（Betelgeuse）；论荣光，算不得天狼星（Sirius），也拿不出天狼伴星（Companion of Sirius）那种矮星的光芒烈度。他燃烧的心被包裹在光球层（photosphere）之内——一层赤热的气体之海，深过整个亚洲，四围被鞭挞成波浪，以及珠穆朗玛般高的绯红浪花的喷柱，从地球上望去足以洒到月亮；色球层（chromosphere）是他氦与氢织成的外氅；日冕由日冕素（coronium）所成；还有那黄道之光（zodiacal light）。他中心的热以百万计，表面的热以千计，皆以度数论。比水银柱更好的指引，是在撒哈拉的的正午、亚丁（Aden）的午后茶时分，去感受他全力照耀的威力，再想到他发给我们的只是它的二十亿分之一，而且是从九千多万英里之外发来的。

===Original===

He has had children. Under most theories of the world’s origin﻿—nebular, tidal, planetesimal, collisional﻿—he is our parent, ancestor, creator; by him we had life. Under all physical theories of the world’s present, by him we live.

*Lucerna Mundi*, lantern of the world, by him we see.

He maintains the seas, the lakes and the rivers. He keeps in gaseous state the air we must breathe, in liquid state the water we and all living things must absorb﻿—so much whereof as we need he vapourizes and turns into clouds, which he drives the winds to distribute for us. He enables the plants to assimilate their food from the air, and so is the author of all fruits, flowers and trees; the wine we drink and the wood we burn are directly informed of him, the warmth they give us is indirectly his. His spots pulsate, and we pulsate in answer. Electricity, radioactivity and their thousand daughter inventions are his; and the force in our brains and in our bodies. In him we live and move and have our being. Without him we are not.

And he is dying.

===Chinese===

他曾生养儿女。在世界起源的诸种理论之下——星云假说、潮汐假说、星子假说、碰撞假说——他是我们的亲长、先祖、造物主；我们因他而得生命。在世界现状的一切物理理论之下，我们因他而活着。
「世界之灯」（*Lucerna Mundi*），世界的灯笼，我们借他而看见。
他维持着海洋、湖泊与江河。他使我们必须呼吸的空气保持气态，使我们和一切生物必须吸收的水保持液态——我们需要多少，他便把多少蒸发成云，再驱使风替我们分送。他使草木得以从空气中摄取养分，因此是一切果实、花卉与树木的创造者；我们饮的酒、我们烧的柴都直接禀受于他，它们给我们的温暖间接也是他的。他的黑子搏动，我们也随之搏动相应。电、放射性，以及它们千般的派生发明，都是他的；我们头脑里、身体里的力量也是。我们生活、动作、存留都在乎他。没有他，我们便什么都不是。
而他正在死去。

===Original===

Through all those asserted certainties﻿—water, fire and the rest﻿—we had come so far to none so absolute. It is a fact no closing of eyes, no denying of objective reality can avert, no mystical-mathematical four-dimensional universe of point-events and curved space-time can exclude, no magical non-dimensional universe of God’s Dream or the Devil’s can abolish; within the framework of every scheme and every schemelessness the same will happen, the sun will die﻿—just as each one of us will die in his time, whatever the grand plan or no-plan of the terrible universe may be.

Optimism seeks to prolong his life a little. Declares him of low density, susceptible of much further compression, able for long ages yet to generate more heat than he radiates. Chemical processes reinforce the contractional one, radioactive processes the chemical: the transmutation of his store of radial elements is a process almost everlasting, ensuring him effluence of light almost stanchless. His positive and negative ions bombard each other, whose mutual annihilation secures their common eternity as heat. Millions of meteors rush hourly upon him, their high speed compensating for their lowly size and making them substantial augmenters and preservers. Above all he is young, still in the bright earlier yellow-heat stage that (on one of the stubborner new theories of stellar evolution) precedes the white-heat of middle life and far precedes the sere later yellow-heat stage, the second childhood, the long late afternoon of stars. Even when at last he should dwindle, the earth would then be moving nearer him, and thus be keeping pace﻿—and keeping warm.

Pessimism curtails it. Density is high; compression peak was passed long ago; generation of new heat has ceased and only radiation is continuing, at the rate of two hundred and fifty million tons a minute, three hundred and sixty thousand million tons a day. If chemically engendered, his warmth has not long to last; a piece of coal is soon burnt out. Though radial transmutation be proceeding, and the loud cannonades of ionization, either is at best an “almost” eternal process; which means nothing, piteous nothing. The meteors are paltry helpers; if he attracted enough of them to be worth while, his size would continually be increasing; and it is not increasing. Above all he is old; astrophysically provable in the second yellow era. Even were he not dwindling away, the earth would always be getting less of his heat, since ever receding from him into the cold outer distance.

Leave all such hopes and counter-hopes. Leave aside the sunspots and other such famous regions of solar controversy. Leave out of account the respective chances of his entering a colder corner of the universe or a warmer﻿—look away altogether from the sun himself. Look back to the earth, or beyond to the stars.

===Chinese===

穿越过那些被断言的必然——水、火等等——我们迄今不曾遇上如此绝对的必然。这是一个事实：闭眼抹杀不了，否认客观现实改变不了，点事件与弯曲时空那神秘-数学的四维宇宙排除不了，上帝之梦或魔鬼之梦那无维度的魔法宇宙也取消不了；在每一种体系、每一种无体系的框架之内，同样的事都要发生，太阳将死去——正如我们每一个人到时候都要死去一样，无论这可怖的宇宙有其宏图大计还是根本无计。
乐观主义想把他寿命稍稍延长。宣称他密度尚低，还能大加压缩，长久岁月里仍能产热多于辐射。化学过程给收缩过程助威，放射过程又给化学过程助威：他库存的放射性元素的蜕变近乎是万世不易的过程，保证他的光流近乎永不枯竭。他的正负离子互相轰击，彼此的湮灭反倒保障它们共同的永恒，化为热。每小时有百万计的流星扑向他，高速弥补了它们的渺小，使它们成为实实在在的增援者与保全者。尤其是他还年轻，仍处在明亮的早期黄热阶段——照恒星演化中一种颇为顽固的新理论——先于中年的白热，更远远先于后来枯槁的黄热阶段、第二童年、恒星漫长的迟暮。即便他终有萎缩之日，届时的地球也正向他移近，从而与他并驾齐驱——也保持温暖。
悲观主义把寿命削减。密度已然很高；压缩的峰值早已过去；新热的生成已经停止，只剩辐射还在继续，速率是每分钟两亿五千万吨，每天三千六百亿吨。倘若他的热是化学所生，那便撑不了多久；一块煤很快就烧完。纵然放射性蜕变在进行，纵然电离的炮声隆隆，充其量也只是个「差不多」永恒的过程；这等于什么都没保证，可怜的什么都没有。流星是无足轻重的帮手；倘若他吸引到的流星多得值得计较，他的体积就该不断增大；可它并没有增大。尤其是他已经老了；用天体物理学便可证明，他正处在第二个黄热纪。即使他不在萎缩，地球也总是得到越来越少的热，因为它正不断退离他，退向寒冷的外围深处。
撇开这一切希望与反希望。撇开太阳黑子与诸如此类著名的太阳争讼之地。撇开他驶入宇宙一隅寒角或暖角的种种或然——干脆把太阳本人移开目光。回望地球，或远望群星。

===Original===

Chronometrically classified by spectral seniority, the stars are known by their age. Like man they pass through seven ages, passing from one to the next with as much choice or chance of rebellion as has he. Whether the sun’s epoch be that of early middle-age or late, the difference is at most between a very little more than half his course to run or a very little less. The whole course must be run, till the seventh age and last.

That the earth was once warmer than now, there is persuasive testimony. Despite cyclical reversions, such as the present warm spell following the last ice age, and even though the ice ages be not caused, or not chiefly caused, by diminution in the solar heat but by this or that wayward difference of ellipticity in the earth’s own orbit or whatnot else﻿—even so, the line of the thermic graph is in the long run always downward. After each glacial era the warm reaction is less warm than the time before. The lull after the Cambrian Ice Age was out of comparison warmer than this. There were no deciduous trees. Could the Coal Forests flourish in South Wales or North France today? Could the reptiles now stretch from pole to pole? As late as the warm centuries after the last ice age but one, no permanent snow and ice remained; the polar caps are brand-new. If Arctic fossils are found embedded in what are now warmer climes, as the famous reindeer of Southern France, tropical fauna and flora are far oftener found in what are now colder regions. The primates, we and the monkeys, originated in the Arctic Circle; Peary found Eden. Greenland was once green; amid her Icy Mountains did not the breadfruit trees once wave? Were not the London tubes, a few yards below our streets, cut through clay that enclosed great tropical shells? Does not the Southern Railway, as it crawls over Kent, but piously emulate its predecessors there, the crocodiles? In Paradise﻿—

===Chinese===

依光谱资历作时计式的分级，恒星的年龄便为人所知。它们像人一样要度过七个年龄，从一个年龄到下一个年龄，其可以选择或可以反抗的机会，同人一样少。无论太阳的纪元是中年前期还是后期，差别至多是：余下的路程略多于一半，或略少于一半。全程终须走完，直到第七个年龄，也是最后一个。
地球曾比今日更暖，此说有令人信服的证据。尽管有周期性的回转，比如上次冰期之后眼下这段温暖期；尽管冰期的成因并非、或主要并非太阳热力的衰减，而是地球自身轨道椭率任性乖张的变化或其他什么名堂——即便如此，温度曲线的长线始终向下。每个冰期过后，回暖都不如上一次暖。寒武纪冰期之后的间歇期，暖得与今日不可同日而语。那时没有落叶树。成煤森林今天还能在南威尔士或北法兰西繁茂么？爬虫今天还能从一极绵延到另一极么？晚到上上次冰期之后的温暖世纪，尚无经年不化的冰雪；两极的冰盖是崭新之物。如果说北极化石也见埋于今日较暖的地带，如南法兰西著名的驯鹿，那么热带的动植物更是屡屡见于今日寒冷的地区。灵长类，我们和猴子，起源于北极圈内；皮尔里（Peary）找到的原来是伊甸园。格陵兰（Greenland）曾经绿过；在她冰封的群山之间，面包树不曾迎风摇曳过么？伦敦的地铁，在街道下不过几码之处，凿穿的黏土里不也封着巨大的热带贝壳么？南方铁路在肯特（Kent）上缓缓爬行时，不正是在虔诚地效法它在那里的前辈——鳄鱼么？在乐园里——

===Original===

Si plain de joye et de solas

				Que nus n’y puet devenir las﻿—

did not the sun, as Adam long afterwards told Seth, shine daily, all day, deliciously? In the Golden Age was not the world warmer and lovelier, while happiness stretched to the far north? Then the flight from the Poles began. It will continue till we huddle on the Equator.

===Chinese===

满溢着欢乐与慰藉（Si plain de joye et de solas）
					无人能在其中生倦意——（Que nus n’y puet devenir las—）
太阳不是照亚当许久之后告诉塞特（Seth）的那样，天天照耀、终日照耀、照得甘美无比么？黄金时代的世界不是更暖也更可爱么，那时幸福一直铺展到极北之地？后来，逃离两极的大迁徙开始了。它将继续下去，直到我们挤作一团蜷在赤道上。

===Original===

Man will fight his fate. He will adapt himself. Even today he can survive in parts of Siberia that in January are no warmer than the moon. He will adapt nature; will learn to utilize the earth’s internal heat, if need be moving underground to become a cave-dweller again, and to economize the sun’s. His industries, by chance and then deliberately, will increase the amount of carbonic gas in the air, and so help the latter to retain heat, increase heat, and distribute it more evenly. Heat thus eked out with a hundred strange inventions, light he will learn to dispense with. In ways we cannot conceive of, any more than the mole or the mastodon could conceive of ours, our descendant race, if still on the planet millions of years from now﻿—Man let us still proudly dub him﻿—will be fighting for his life; every secret of terrible science, every trick of his terrified brain, will be called into play to hold up, perhaps for ages, the inevitable end.

Inevitable. As the sun’s light and warmth grow less, the means of artificially conserving them will grow more complicated, and after a while less efficient. By when, despite all strugglings, all undreamt marvels, all delays, the temperature must have fallen by forty or fifty degrees everywhere, earth will have changed from anything we can imagine, will have dwindled to a belt of Equatorial Eskimos white-girdling the icebound tropics. Dimness of anguish; dayfall of the world. God will have fulfilled His grim promises: He will have darkened the sun in his going forth, He will have made their vintage shouting to cease.

===Chinese===

人会与命运搏斗。他会调适自己。即使在今天，他也能在西伯利亚一些一月里不比月亮暖和的地方活下来。他会调适自然；会学会利用地球内部的热，必要时迁入地下、重新做起穴居人，并节省太阳的热。他的工业，起于偶然、继而出自有意，将增加空气中碳酸气的含量，从而帮助空气保住热、增多热、更均匀地分配热。热既靠百般奇巧的发明勉强维持，光他将学会省去。以我们无法设想的方式——鼹鼠或乳齿象设想不出我们的方式，我们也设想不出后人的——我们后代的族类，倘若千百万年后仍在行星之上——让我们仍旧骄傲地称他为人——将为生存而战；可怖科学的每一项秘密，他惊惶头脑的每一样机巧，都将被动员起来，把那不可避免的终局，或许一撑便是许多个世代。
终究不可避免。随着太阳的光与热日渐减少，人工保全它们的手段将愈发繁复，再过一阵便愈发无效。到那时，尽管有一切挣扎、一切梦想不到的奇迹、一切拖延，各地的气温必已下降四五十度，地球将变得全然出乎我们的想象，缩成一条赤道爱斯基摩人的环带，白茫茫地箍住冰封的热带。苦痛的昏冥；世界的日沉。上帝将兑现他冷峻的应许：他将使日头一出便昏暗，将使他们的丰收欢呼止息。

===Original===

Down by sixty or seventy, there will be no life more; and earth a bleak charnel-house, a whited sepulchre. The oceans, if still existent, will be of ice; the clouds, if still any, will be of snow. No wind, no warmth, no rain; no flesh wherein is the breath of life. The cold and the darkness.

The last man will have lain long unburied across the equator of the ageing wizened globe, long have forgotten the story of splendour and sorrow of the strange race that perished with him, long have forgotten the dwindling purple sun his eyes beheld in the moment when they closed forever.

===Chinese===

再降六七十度，便再无生命；大地成了一座凄凉的藏骸所，一座粉饰的坟墓。海洋，倘若还在，将是冰；云，倘若还有，将是雪。无风，无暖，无雨；没有带着生命之气息的血肉。寒冷与黑暗。
最后一个人将横卧在衰老干瘪的地球赤道上，久不得埋葬，早已忘却与他一同灭亡的那个奇异族类的荣华与哀伤的故事，早已忘却他双眼永远合上那一刻所望见的、那轮萎缩发紫的太阳。

---

			

===Original===

Like the frozen corpse, or vespillo of corpses, still whirling around him, the sun in his turn will die. A solid crust will form; gases will become oceans, molten lava dry land. Death will come upon him more quickly; he will receive no vivifying rays from outside, as the earth did from him; the sun will have no sun. More swiftly will his oceans turn to ice, his atmosphere descend on him as a pall of fine white snow. Seen from far off﻿—seen by Whom?﻿—he will change from gold to vespertine yellow, to red, to dark purple; then to sackcloth of hair, frozen black.

When he too is gone, with whatever race of beings his cooling-time may have witnessed upon him, the last men of earth will countless myriads of years have been dead. He mourned them, perhaps; himself will die threneless.

===Chinese===

正像那至今仍绕着他旋舞的冻结尸身——或者说，诸尸身的执绋者（vespillo）——太阳自己也终将死去。一层固体地壳将结成；气体将成为海洋，熔岩成为旱地。死亡将更快地降临于他；他不会从外界收到赋生的光线，像地球曾从他那里收到的那样；太阳将没有自己的太阳。他的海洋将更快地化为冰，他的大气将更疾地披落在他身上，成了一件细白雪花织成的尸衣。从远处望去——被谁望着呢？——他将从金色变为黄昏的黄色，变为红色，变为暗紫；然后变得黑如毛发织的麻衣，冻结的黑。
待到他也熄灭——连同他冷却的岁月里可能在他身上见证过的无论什么族类——地球上的最后一批人早已死去无数万古了。他或许为他们哀悼过；他自己死去时，将无挽歌。

### Crash / 碰撞

===Original===

But Earth herself? Who, for all the harm frost could do her, might go on travelling round the dead sun forever.

The globe too, this orb of solid matter, will have an end; whether early, in time to preclude man’s separate dooms, or late, infinite ages after he has gone. Which end is collision, impact, dissolving crash.

===Chinese===

可是大地她自己呢？任凭严霜能加她多少戕害，她仍尽可以绕着那颗死去的太阳，永远旅行下去。

这颗地球也一样——这团固态物质之球，终将有个尽头；或是早的，早得来得及拦在人类各自的劫数之先；或是晚的，晚到人去之后无穷万古。而这一尽头，便是相撞，便是冲撞，便是消解一切的轰然崩坠。

===Original===

Not probably with a comet. Though that derided chance, being also in the nature of a concussion, a catastrophe, may lift up high again its fiery head.

Nor with a meteor. No bolide or shooting-star ever experienced has been large enough to do the world grave damage, leave alone demolish it. No bethel ever fallen from heaven and erected on earth has stood higher than three of its worshippers; not great Diana of the Ephesians, nor the baetylical idol of Zeus Teleios at Tegea, nor Jacob’s pillar of thanksgiving at Luz-Bethel itself. That famous stone that fell in Phrygia, and was raised to godhead as Cybele, measured a yard or two. That scar in the Painted Desert, though the mightiest meteoric consequence alleged, is not half-a-mile in radius, not a furlong deep. There may of course be meteor-troops many times more numerous, with each trooper many times bigger, than anything deducible either from past knowledge, or from present acquaintance with friendly Aquarids and Gremenids and Leonids; since meteorites come from outside the solar system, how affirm anything absolute, whether as to their maximum size or their maximum speed? The meteor death-roll through history is so far two: a peasant once in India, a cow long ago in Brazil.

===Chinese===

多半不会是撞上彗星。不过那遭人哂笑的机缘，既然骨子里也属一场震荡、一场灾变，说不定还会把它烈焰的头颅重新高高昂起。

也不会是撞上流星。历来经历过的火流星（bolide）与陨星，从不曾大到足以重创世界，更遑论将它摧毁。凡从天上坠下、在地上竖起的圣石（bethel），没有一块立得高过它的三个敬拜者：不是以弗所人的大女神狄安娜（great Diana of the Ephesians），不是忒革亚（Tegea）奉成全的宙斯（Zeus Teleios）的那尊圣石神像（baetylical idol），也不是路斯-伯特利（Luz-Bethel）本地雅各立下的感恩石柱。那块坠落在弗里吉亚（Phrygia）、被尊奉为库柏勒（Cybele）神格的著名石头，量起来不过一两码。彩色沙漠（Painted Desert）里那道疤痕，纵然号称陨石造就的最宏大后果，半径也不足半英里，深不足一弗隆（furlong）。当然，流星的大军也许比从以往的知识、或从眼下与友善的宝瓶座流星雨（Aquarids）、双子座流星雨（Gemenids）、狮子座流星雨（Leonids）的交往中所能推想的一切，还要众多上许多倍，每一员兵卒还要庞大上许多倍；既然陨石来自太阳系之外，又怎能断言什么绝对之事——无论其体积的极限，还是其速度的极限？有史以来陨石的死亡名单，至今共两名：印度的一个农夫，很久以前巴西的一头母牛。

===Original===

Nor with a planet. Their courses are ordered like our own, and will remain separate and regular while ours does.

Perhaps with the sun. If we his satellites, moving ever quicker, and ever nearer him, should one day fall on him bodily.

===Chinese===

也不会是撞上行星。它们的行径同我们的一样受着天律的编排；我们的轨道保持常态多久，它们也将彼此分立、循规蹈矩多久。

或许是撞上太阳。倘若我们这些他的卫星，转得愈来愈快、离他愈来愈近，有朝一日整个儿坠落在他的身上。

===Original===

Probably with the moon. The Sagas knew it; it was revealed to the Prophet at Mecca. Of all the stories, as of all the theories, this one alone has found acceptance of pure mathematics, elsewhere so scornful; this one alone has been prophetically worked out to the last omega of the last perfect formula; if mathematics is valid, this is valid. For the solar tides are lengthening the period of rotation of the earth, till one day, some five hundred thousand million years from now, it must become longer than the period of rotation of the moon. This latter will then be retarded. After her untold ages of recession the moon will stand still. Then, very slowly, begin to return. By when in our sight she is equal to twenty suns, the fierce tides we shall raise on her will break her body up. She will bombard us with lunar pieces two miles, ten miles thick. The whole side of her facing the earth will burst open. She will torrent forth streams of white lava, liquid fragments larger than Sicily, to burn, bury and ravish the whole world’s face. She will split in two. Then the two halves in two, then again﻿—soon into a wild skyful of dwarf moons, moving ever closer to each other and to us, and forming at the last, to enshroud us, entomb us and adorn, a Saturn’s ring tight-encircling the earth.

===Chinese===

多半是撞上月亮。萨迦（Sagas）早知道这件事；麦加的先知也曾蒙启示。在一切故事之中，正如在一切理论之中，唯独这一个博得了纯粹数学的认可——那在别处何等倨傲轻蔑的纯粹数学；唯独这一个已被推演到先知般周备，直到那最后一个完美公式的最后一枚俄梅戛（omega）；数学若有效，这一个便有效。因为太阳潮汐正拉长地球的自转周期，直到有朝一日——距今约五千亿年之后——它势必变得比月亮的旋转周期更长。届时，后者将转而被拖慢。在她那说不尽的万古退行之后，月亮将凝立不动。然后，极缓极缓地，开始回返。等到她在我们眼中已抵得上二十个太阳，我们在她身上掀起的凶猛潮汐便会将她的身躯撕裂。她将用两英里厚、十英里厚的月亮碎块轰击我们。她面向地球的整面将轰然迸裂。她将喷涌出白色熔岩的湍流，比西西里（Sicily）更大的液体碎块，来焚烧、掩埋、蹂躏整个世界的脸面。她将裂成两半。两半再各裂成两半，再裂——霎时间化作满天狂乱的侏儒众月，彼此愈靠愈近，也向我们愈靠愈近，最后结成一道紧紧环抱地球的土星环，来裹殓我们、葬埋我们，并为我们添妆。

---

			

===Original===

Assuredly with a star. From the heavenly host will come the last charge of all, the ultimate cosmic concussion, and terminal collision of astral annihilation.

Sooner or later, in process of unwearying time (time that goes on forever), each star of heaven encounters each other star. Observed collisions are not rare; collisions unobservable from Earth must be taking place continually in the unsearched depths of the universe. One day, sooner or later, unobservable from the unsearched depths of the universe, our own collision will take place: our sun, with his whole solisequious convoy, rammed into steam by another.

Later, it may be.

===Chinese===

必是撞上一颗恒星。从天军（heavenly host）之中，将发起最后的总冲锋，那宇宙的终极震荡，那星辰湮灭的末日对撞。

或迟或早，在不倦的时间（那永远流转的时间）的进程中，天上每一颗恒星都要与每一颗别的恒星相逢。观测到的碰撞并不稀罕；从地球上观测不到的碰撞，想必正在宇宙不可探测的深处接连发生。总有一天，或迟或早——宇宙不可探测的深处所观测不到的——我们自己的碰撞也将发生：我们的太阳，连同他整支逐日而行的（solisequious）车队，被另一颗恒星撞成蒸汽。

也可能，是迟。

===Original===

The “crowded streets of space” are streets of voidness, and today, as it befalls, we are journeying along a particularly empty and uninhabited wide road. Dark unseen suns, once thought more numerous than seen ones, have, by new gravitational calculations on the speed of the latter, been reduced to the pure darkness of non-being. Of the seen ones, our three nearest neighbours, triune companions in the Centaur, are twenty-five billion miles away; the next nearest forty billion. In a circumference of some two hundred and fifty billion miles there are but four stars of us﻿—room to move without jostling.

None seeks to jostle. Like bees in a swarm, whom they truly resemble, stars keep out of each other’s way. From the nearest one fast moving upon us we are moving away as fast. The star we are making for, scared Vega in the Lyre, is making away in her turn. A star’s chance of collision, opines one authority (whose equations are given), is one in six hundred thousand billion, or six followed by seventeen noughts. We are as likely to crash (declares another doctor, whose calculations are however withheld) as two balloons sent up at different ends of Russia.

===Chinese===

太空的「闹街」（“crowded streets of space”）原是空无的街衢；而今日，恰逢其会，我们正旅行在一条格外空旷、人烟全无的宽广大道上。黑暗不可见的太阳，一度被认为多于可见者，如今凭着对后者速度的新的引力计算，已被削减为纯粹不存在之黑。在可见者之中，我们最近的三个邻居——半人马座（the Centaur）里的三位一体旅伴——远在二十五万亿英里之外；其次最近的一颗，在四十万亿英里外。在周长约二百五十万亿英里的圆周之内，只有我们四颗星——尽有余裕往来而不致推挤。

谁也不寻衅推挤。恒星们像蜂群里的蜜蜂——它们真正肖似的就是蜜蜂——彼此互相让路。离我们最近的那颗星正疾速向我们扑来，而我们正以同等的疾速退离它。我们正驶向的那颗星，天琴座（the Lyre）里受惊的织女星（Vega），这会儿也在转身逃开。一颗恒星遭逢碰撞的机会，据一位权威（其方程式业已给出）之见，是六十万万亿分之一，也就是 6 后面缀着 17 个零。我们相撞的可能（另一位博士宣称，其计算却未曾示人），不比从俄国两端放起的两只气球更大。

===Original===

The stars have their orbits like the planets; on some regular plan, unknown but harmonious, their movements through the celestial spaces are adjusted. A collision only happens when something goes wrong: how often, how imminent, is that?

Sooner, it may be.

===Chinese===

恒星们也像行星一样各有自己的轨道；它们穿越诸天空间的运行，是照着某种有常的方案——未知而和谐——调停停当的。只有出了岔子，碰撞才会发生：岔子这回事——多常有？多迫近？

也可能，是早。

===Original===

The streets of space *are* crowded; they are strewn with continual catastrophe. Of the seen stars, few no doubt at this instant are in our immediate vicinage. But the tramp stars﻿—dark suns wandering unglimpsed through the void, droves of dead worlds no equations can abolish﻿—are everywhere, more numerous than the lucid ones, as dead men than live; and it is a tramp star that our sun, then like to be a tramp star himself, is likest to encounter.

Not all the stars, live or dead, can be moving away from each other. In space Euclidean or non-Euclidean, in commonsense four-dimensional or three, as many must be moving nearer each other; towards disaster as away from it.

===Chinese===

太空的街道确实拥挤；它们遍撒着接连不断的灾变。此时此刻，紧邻我们左近的可见恒星无疑寥寥无几。可那些流浪星（tramp stars）——暗黑的太阳，无人瞥见地漂泊在虚空之中，成群死去的世界，任何方程式都勾销不得——却无处不在，其数多于光明者，一如死人多于活人；而我们的太阳——届时不免自己也成了一颗流浪星——最可能遭逢的，正是一颗流浪星。

所有的星，无论生死，不可能全都在彼此退开。在欧几里得或非欧几里得的空间里，在常识的四维或三维里，必有同样多的正在彼此趋近；奔向灾变的，同逃离灾变的一样多。

===Original===

Some order in the star-streams, harmony of the heavens, music of the spheres, is predictable; the Empyrean as a perfect mechanism without flaw or disturbance is not. Disasters disprove it. Often, and imminently, something goes wrong.

Any moment we may strike a nebular stretch of heaven. Such regions are full of solid matter, stardust, in-drifting meteorites and the like. The nebula, acting as a brake on the sun’s motion, will reduce his pace at the very instant he enters an area where, other bodies being more numerous, pace to escape them is what he will chiefly need.

Nor, principally, is head-on collision requisite. Let another great star pass near﻿—one thousand million miles away. He will derange, de-orbit us; send us crashing, liquefying, into the riven and distorted sun. Let Antares or Arcturus, of terrible size and terrible speed, move by at tenfold, twelvefold the solar distance. The sun will be pulled into streamers, tearing forth to greet the Arcturian dragons of fire; in half-an-hour we shall be fused out of all identity, a drop in their chaos. And many a star is mightier than Arcturus; and many are nearer; many faster. One in Columba runs at two million miles an hour.

===Chinese===

星流中的几分秩序，诸天的和谐，天球的乐音（music of the spheres），是可以预测的；至高天（the Empyrean）作为一台无瑕无扰的完美机械，则不可。灾变否证了它。岔子常出，而且迫在眉睫。

任何时刻，我们都可能撞上天宇的一片星云地带。这样的区域里满是固态物质、星尘、飘聚而来的陨石之类。星云充作太阳行进的刹车，恰恰在他驶入这样一片天区的当口放慢他的脚步——而在那里，别的天体更为众多，他最急需的恰恰是逃开它们的脚程。

况且，头对头的迎面相撞，也并非主要的必需。只消另一颗大恒星从近旁驶过——在十亿英里之外。他便会搅乱我们，把我们拽出轨道；把我们摔进、熔进那被扯裂而歪扭的太阳。只消心宿二（Antares）或大角星（Arcturus）——那可怕的大小、可怕的速度——从十倍、十二倍日地距离处驶过。太阳将被拉成道道流苏，撕裂着扑出去迎候大角星的火龙；不出半个钟头，我们便将熔得形迹全消，化作它们混沌中的一滴。而比大角更强的恒星，有的是；更近的，有的是；更快的，有的是。天鸽座（Columba）里就有一颗，正以每小时二百万英里的速度狂奔。

---

			

===Original===

Sooner or later, assuredly with a star.

Yet collision, which thus includes grazing and mere propinquity, is too narrow a word.

Collision, which implies splintering impact of solid bodies, is too massive a word.

Collision, which, in lubberly portrayal of unliving earths in lumbering percussion, conjures up no vision of the dying crying souls upon them, is too abstract, too astronomical a word.

===Chinese===

或迟或早，必是撞上一颗恒星。

然而「碰撞」——既然它如今连擦掠与仅仅的毗邻都一并收罗在内——是个太窄的词。

「碰撞」——它意味着固体天体迸碎的相击——是个太重的词。

「碰撞」——它笨拙地描画着无生气的诸地球迟缓呆板的互撞，唤不起那上面垂死哀号的灵魂的半点景象——是个太抽象、太天文学的词。

===Original===

Collision, the one fate that will outstay the others, yet partakes of the nature of them all. Like the cold, irrevocable. Like the comet, coming at an hour unknown, from a direction unknown; a chance, not a process; a crash, not a geological creeping. Resembling the water-end or the waterless, according as the foe’s first deed would be to melt the mountain snows and flood us, or to parch. Resembling the fire-end; it is the Fire-End, destinate, ultimate, Sheol-Gehenna, the great balefire of combustion.

Collision, the one fate that destroys not only man but his home, alone is destroyer and creator. The twin cyclopean gas-streams, as they flare out across the furious infinite, will take the form of a double spiral; then of a gaseous nebula; then, condensing, of a star﻿—maybe with planets, with an earth, with us all reborn again, again long after to re-die. Death nova will be birth nova, as the nebula which gave us life was born itself of death, in the crash and ruin of life preceding. The heavenly colossal catastrophes are death; they are life. Collision makes; collision kills.

===Chinese===

「碰撞」，那将比其他诸劫存续得更久的唯一劫数，却兼禀它们全体的本性。像寒冷一样，无可挽回。像彗星一样，在未知的时辰、从未知的方向降临；是机缘，不是过程；是轰然一崩，不是地质学式的缓缓爬行。像水之终局，或像无水之终——全看那仇敌的第一桩行事，是化开山巅的积雪来灌淹我们，还是来烤焦。像火之终局；它就是那火之终局（the Fire-End）——命定的、终极的，阴间—欣嫩谷（Sheol-Gehenna），燃烧的冲天凶燔。

「碰撞」，那不仅毁灭人、连人的家园也一并毁灭的唯一劫数，唯独它既是毁灭者，又是创造者。那一对独眼巨人般的气体洪流，当它们在狂怒的无垠中迸散开来，将化作一道双重旋涡；继而化作一团气态星云；继而凝缩而成一颗恒星——也许带着行星，带着一颗地球，带着我们全体重新降生，此后良久，再度死去。死亡的新星将是诞生的新星，一如当年赋予我们生命的星云，本身也生于死亡，生于先行生命的碰撞与毁败。诸天的滔天大劫是死亡；它们也是生命。碰撞创造；碰撞杀戮。

===Original===

Out of the chance riot of stars came worlds, came living existence, came we. In new chance riot we shall depart. The heavens go crashing and whirling. The universe forms, un-forms, re-forms. The Terror rolls on. What is it? What means it? What is my soul in that universe? May God have mercy.

===Chinese===

从群星的偶然暴乱里，出来了诸世界，出来了生命存在，出来了我们。在新的偶然暴乱里，我们将要离去。诸天轰撞着、旋卷着。宇宙成形、解形、再成形。恐怖滚滚向前。它是什么？它意味着什么？在那宇宙之中，我的灵魂是什么？愿上帝垂怜。

### God / 上帝

===Original===

Will He have mercy?

Impiously our attention has all been devoted to modes of material destroying of a material world, as though no Spirit breathed through it; atheistically, only to so-called natural ways of termination.

There are supernatural ways. In these men have believed; each religion, each sect, each sub-sect holding its different guess as to His choice of hour, contributing its variant as to His choice of method, ready to fight or to die for its own pet terminal detail, ready to murder and martyr for a deviation or a doubt. In these men still believe. If no one now or had ever believed in them, we could not with our pygmy frail knowledge and paltry five senses rule them out.

Astrologers have in all ages foretold the Birthday Death: “when the fixed stars have made a revolution unto the points from whence they first set out, a kind of dying upon the day of its Nativity”﻿—prophets and peoples the End as Punishment: the natural forms of destruction, fire, flood or ice, being supernaturally sent of God, or the gods, by way of rebuke and recompense for the multitude of our iniquity, for three transgressions of Damascus and for four﻿—magicians, saints and mystics the Decreation: the sudden unframing of the worlds by the word of Him Who framed them: *Fiat*, a shake of the sceptre, and then Nothing.

===Chinese===

祂可会垂怜？

不敬地，我们的注意力至今全都倾注在以种种物质方式毁灭一个物质世界上，仿佛没有一缕灵息吹拂其间；无神地，只倾注于所谓「自然的」终局之途。

超自然的途径是有的。人们一向信这些；每种宗教、每个教派、每个支派，都各持一种关于祂择定时辰的猜度，各献一种关于祂选用方法的变说，随时准备为自己偏爱的一项末世细节而战、而死，随时准备为一处异说或一丝疑念去杀人、去殉道。人们如今仍信这些。纵使如今无人、且从来无人信过它们，凭我们侏儒般脆弱的知识与寒碜的五感，也无权将它们排除在外。

历代的占星家都预言过「诞辰之死」（the Birthday Death）：「当诸恒星运转一周、回到它们当初出发的点位，便是一种死在诞生之日的死」——先知与万民则把末日当作惩罚：毁灭的种种自然形式——火、洪水或冰——乃由上帝、或诸神超自然地降下，以责斥并报偿我们罪愆之繁多，为大马士革的三次过犯、又为四次——术士、圣徒与神秘家则预言「去创造」（the Decreation）：立起诸世界的那一位，以一言将它们骤然拆散：「要有」（*Fiat*），权杖一振，而后便是虚无。

===Original===

God is the self and essence of each of the elements whose temporal manifestations have here been humbly unfolded. He is water, He is ice; Himself is the stars; our God is a consuming fire. He will act through these His elements not modally, to human sense perceptibly; but essentially, without circumstance, extrinsicality or phase. As it was in the beginning, when all was without form and void, till His Spirit moved upon the face of the unborn waters,

A moving mist,
					

					A quickness which my God hath kist,

and hatched the world, so likewise shall it be in the end: the Spirit of God shall move upon the face of the living waters, unkiss, unquicken, decreate them, and all shall be without form, and void. As mysteriously as it began the dream will end, the mirage tumble. A magical moment, and then Nothing.

===Chinese===

上帝就是其时间性的显现已在此谦卑铺陈过的那每一种元素的自性与本质。祂是水，祂是冰；祂自身就是众星；我们的上帝乃是烈火。祂行于祂这些元素之中，非循样式、非为人类感官所可觉察地行；而是循本质，无境况、无外在、无阶段。正如太初之时，万象无形而空虚，直到祂的灵运行在未生之水的面上，

一阵浮动的雾，
					一种我的上帝亲吻过的灵机，

于是孵出了这个世界；末日也将同样如此：上帝的灵将运行在活水的面上，收回亲吻，收回生机，将它们「去创造」，万有都将归于无形与空虚。这梦怎样神秘地开始，也将怎样神秘地终结，蜃景倾颓。一个魔法的瞬息，而后便是虚无。

---

			

===Original===

The strange modern people do not think so. The normal (abnormal) Western man of 1930 who swims abreast of the currents of his time, who accepts the typical teaching of his day and generation or who, learned or lewd, awarely or unawares, is influenced by it, probably does not think so. The Great Naturalistic Revolution of the past hundred years has not changed﻿—nor will the Great Relativity Revolution of the next hundred years succeed in changing﻿—the unchanged and unchangeable. Yet for some minds it has changed some large aspects of the changeable, and has given them a new conception, as “right” or as “wrong” as the conceptions from the caveman onwards that preceded it, of finite things: how these are likely, though not certain, to behave; what is their most feasible physical future. According to this conception, though the Transcendental may be there, It will not, cannot, intervene irregularly or arbitrarily in the ordered working of the given universe; in particular It will not, by miracle or deed magical, intervene in the even tenor of any finite entity, such as our own particular Milky Way or solar system or planet﻿—amongst other things to destroy it suddenly. Cannot and will not. The earth and its creatures will perish, as they arose, as part of the normal course of apparent physical nature; by so-styled natural ends, not miraculous.

There is no necessary conflict. Belief in the supernatural nature of the universe need not preclude belief in the natural end of this world. Belief in aboriginal God, or in the soul as a phenomenon not born of nor bound up with matter, or in unseen worlds beyond the seen one, need not exclude the expectation that the seen one will end, or appear to end, in one of such six ways as set forth in these six chapters﻿—by cold or crash or comet or whichever humanly predictable, materially describable, agency it may be. Not everyone unbelieving in Time is indifferent to what will seem to take place within its apparent limits. Not everyone convinced of spiritual reality, or of some unknown form of reality beyond spiritual reality, is convinced that physical reality is meaningless and the disappearance of its familiar shapes a fact of no interest, a phenomenon of no probability. Not everyone persuaded that only mind exists is untouched by the manner of matter’s going. Not every Dreamer scorns how the Dream will fade. Though God is God, and the Mystery unconjecturable, how and when It will put off its present garments is not unworthy man’s conjecture, a speculation with which natural science, as much as any other scheme or system, may profitably and prophetically concern herself.

===Chinese===

奇怪的今人不这么想。1930 年那位与时代潮流并排泅泳的寻常（反常的）西方人，那位接受其时代与其世代之典型教导、或者无论博学抑或鄙俚、自觉抑或不自觉都受其影响的人，多半不这么想。过去百年的自然主义大革命不曾改变——将来百年的相对论大革命也休想改变——那不曾改变且不可改变者。然而对某些头脑而言，它确已改变了可改变之事的一些重大方面，并给了他们一种新的概念，一种关于有限事物的概念，与穴居人以降先于它的诸种概念同样地「对」或同样地「错」：这些事物虽非确定、却大概率会如何行止；它们最可行的物理前途是什么。依照这一概念，超然者（the Transcendental）纵然在那里，它也不会、也不能不规则地或任意地干预既定宇宙的有序运转；尤其是，它不会凭借奇迹或魔法之行事，去干预任何有限存在的平稳行程——例如我们自己这个特定的银河、太阳系或行星——比方说，将其骤然毁灭。不能，也不会。地球及其众生将如它们兴起时那样，作为表观物理自然之正常进程的一部分而消亡；以所谓自然的终局，而非奇迹。

这里并无必然的冲突。相信宇宙的超自然本性，未必排斥相信这个世界的自然终局。相信太初的上帝，或相信灵魂乃一种非生于物质、亦不系于物质的现象，或相信可见世界之外另有不可见的世界，未必排除这样的预期：可见的这一个将以这六章所铺陈的六种方式之一告终，或看来告终——以寒冷、以碰撞、以彗星，或以无论何种人类可预测、物质上可描述的营力。不信时间的人，并非人人都对表观上发生于其限度之内的事物漠不关心。确信属灵实在、或确信属灵实在之外另有某种未知形式之实在的人，并非人人都确信物理实在毫无意义、其熟悉形相的消失无足轻重、绝无可能。信服唯有心存在的人，并非人人为物质离去的方式无动于衷。并非每个做梦者都轻看这大梦（Dream）将如何消褪。上帝虽是上帝，奥秘虽不可臆测，它将于何时、以何种方式卸下现今的衣裳，并非不配人类一猜——这门玄思，自然科学与任何别的方案或体系一样，尽可有益地、且如先知般地为之劳神。

===Original===

Her type of guess may be the right one, and frost, flame or flood the end, the end of soul with body; the conclusion, together with the world visible, of the world invisible of good and evil, joy and suffering, love and terror, that has filled and formed every heart that has beaten or yet shall beat.

Or the magical doubters, and by their side the fundamentalists of all faiths and ages, and maybe (when the Wheel of Knowledge turns full circle) the new philosophers the newer physics will give birth to: these others may be right. Science may be a set of symbols corresponding to no reality whatever; natural law a phantasm unreliable from one hour to the next even within its own imaginary world; cause and effect, time and space, a vicious round of false notions which can never explain a world, leave alone a universe, into which they do not enter; the universe, made up of something beyond both soul and matter, a thing unpredicable and unpredictable in terms of either﻿—as God ineffable, or as Chaos finally irrational. The little world we think we see may have subsisted and subsist forever and forever; the whole cosmos vanish tonight.

===Chinese===

她这一类的猜想也许是对的：霜、焰或洪水便是终局，灵魂与身体同尽的终局；那不可见的世界——善与恶、欢乐与苦难、爱与恐怖的世界，那充满并塑成了每一颗已经跳动过或尚待跳动的心——也将与可见的世界一同了结。

再不然，便是那些信魔法的怀疑论者对了，还有站在他们一边的、一切信仰一切时代的基要主义者（fundamentalists），也许还有（当知识之轮转满一圈时）更新的物理学将孕育出的新哲学家们：这些别样的人也可能是对的。科学也许只是一套与任何实在都毫不对应的符号；自然律也许是一个幽灵，即便在它自己的想象世界里，也从这一个钟点到下一个钟点都靠不住；因果与时空，也许是一环恶性循环的伪观念，永远解释不了一个世界，更遑论一个宇宙——它们根本进不到那里面去；宇宙也许由某种既超乎灵魂也超乎物质的东西构成，一件无法以二者中任何一者来断言、来预言的东西——如上帝之不可言说，或如混沌之终归无理。我们自以为看见的那个小小世界，也许一直存在并将永远存在下去；而整个宇宙，也许就在今夜消失。

## When? / 何时？

===Original===
Fear deferred maketh the heart sick.

When the loved one, the one lover, lies in peril, the clutching dread that precedes is crueller than the long sorrow that follows.

And as terror of things to come is more than all grief for things gone past, so time, whether past or to come, is more terrible than space, Eternity more awful than the abyss. Who would not rather know﻿—rather not know﻿—when he will die than how or where he will die?

===Chinese===
所惧怕的迟迟不来，使人心忧伤。

当所爱之人、那独一无二的爱人身陷险境时，事前攫住人心的恐惧，比事后漫长的悲哀更为残酷。

正如对未来之事的恐怖，甚于对既往之事的一切哀伤，时间——无论过去还是未来——也比空间更可怖，永恒比深渊更可畏。谁不宁愿知道——抑或宁愿不知道——自己何时会死，而非如何死、死于何处？

===Original===
And as for ourselves and our beloved, so likewise with our qualms for the world. Man has ever concerned himself more passionately with the hour than with the way of destruction.

Which problem, if it holds greater interest, holds also greater difficulty. Surmise now moves from the humdrum sphere of matter into the wild realm of number, there to cower among kabbalistic patterns of fixed dates and patternless rows of cold zeros, or among the terrors, beyond all designs and digits, of the dateless zeroless Everlasting. How? was a hard query with many answers. Each of these adds its own complications to the questions When? Soon or late? What notice? At what stage of humanity’s career? Each several mode of the world’s ending brings several guesses at its time. The possibilities are added and multiplied, like the Biblical sums and astronomical ciphers themselves.

===Chinese===
我们对自己、对所爱之人的忧虑如此，对世界的隐忧亦复如是。人类对毁灭时辰的关切，从来都比对毁灭方式的关切更为热切。

这个问题固然更有兴味，却也更为艰难。揣度至此便从物质那平淡的领域，转入数字那狂野的国度：在那里，它要么瑟缩于卡巴拉图式的固定日期之间、瑟缩于毫无图式、唯有冰冷零号的行列之间，要么便匍匐于那超出一切设计与数位的恐怖之前——无日期、无零号的永恒。「怎样？」是个难题，答案众多；而每一种答案又给下列问题各自添上新的纠葛：何时？早还是迟？有何预告？在人类历程的哪一阶段？世界的每一种终结方式，都带来对终结时刻的若干种猜测。可能性相加又相乘，一如圣经里的那些数目与天文数字本身。

### This Year / 今年

===Original===
The end has endlessly been predicted for a given year, named and numbered.

Such predictions cannot altogether be classed with those of a near end rather than a distant, because the predicted date has not, when predicted, always been a near one. From another standpoint such predictions must be classed apart: in their support, almost alone, arguments from physical science are not invoked; today, as in the past, they are the unaided work of those in fee to the magic of numbers, whom if Science is not called upon to help, nor can she hinder. At the ultimate hour, when the fatal (say) star is upon us, enemy aid will doubtless be welcomed﻿—if it chooses to confirm the chosen date. The astrologers then will allow the astronomers, in humble confirmation, to calculate the star’s mass and class, its composition, its rate of advance. Till then the date-givers will ignore their rivals, and continue to pore over their abacuses and swanpans and horoscopes and sibylline books. Till then the scientists will denounce their rivals as charlatans, and continue to pore over their equations and test-tubes and microscopes and statistical charts.

---

===Chinese===
世界的终结曾被无穷无尽地预言于某个指定的年份——指名道姓、编了号的年份。

这类预言并不能全然归入「主张终结临近」而非「主张终结遥远」的一类，因为被预言的日期在预言之时未必总是近的。而从另一个角度看，这类预言又必须另归一类：几乎唯独它们，是完全不求助于自然科学论证的；今日一如往昔，它们全然出自那些受雇于数字魔法之人的手笔——对于这些人，科学既未被请去帮忙，也无力阻拦。到了最后一刻，当那颗（比如说）夺命的星辰逼临头顶，敌营的援手想必也会受欢迎——只要它肯证实那选定的日期。到那时，占星家们将容许天文学家们以谦卑的旁证身份，去计算那颗星的质量与类型、它的成分、它的行进速率。在那之前，赐予日期的人们将无视他们的对手，继续埋头于他们的算架、算盘、星命盘和西卜林书卷。在那之前，科学家们将痛斥他们的对手是江湖骗子，继续埋头于他们的方程式、试管、显微镜和统计图表。

---

===Original===
The Great Sacred Number of the Babylonians was 12,960,000, giving a date similar to some yesterday’s estimates of the sun’s life. Buddhism, early and late, has had many notational guesses. Jewish Apocalyptic, a stream from the waters of Babylon, flows from the Captivity to far beyond the Dispersion with a ceaseless glitter of Rabbinical numbers.

The stream flowed through into the nascent empire of Christianity, which it watered abundantly. Hardly had He uttered it, ere Jesus Christ’s “at hand” was fitted to figures. One year after another of “this generation” was proclaimed the *Dies Irae*.

When the Twelve had gone to their expectation above without seeing it realized here below; when Paul of Tarsus, his imminent hope fast dwindling through his Epistles, had been martyred for the Lord Whose Second Coming, like the First, he saw not; when the Great Persecution had raged over, and the Holy City fallen, and all the signs been accomplished save only the End﻿—then the new religion had to fall back on other explanations and on other dates.

===Chinese===
巴比伦人的大圣数是 12,960,000 ，由此得出的日期，与昨日某些关于太阳寿命的估算相差无几。佛教，无论早期晚期，都做过许多记数式的猜测。犹太启示文学这条出自巴比伦水系的溪流，从被掳之期一路流淌，远过大流散之后，沿途闪动着拉比数目字不息的粼光。

这股水流又灌注到基督教这个新生的帝国里，把它浇灌得十分丰沛。耶稣基督的「近了」话音未落，就已被套上了数目。「这一世代」的一年又一年，相继被宣布为《末日经》之年。

当十二使徒带着他们的盼望升到上面去，却没有在下面看见它实现；当大数的保罗——他那急迫的指望在几封书信里迅速消退——已为那位主殉道，而主的第二次降临如同第一次降临，他都未曾看见；当大逼迫已经肆虐而过，圣城已经陷落，一切征兆都已应验、唯独终结除外——这时，这个新宗教便只得退而依靠别的解释、别的日期。

===Original===
For a while the year 195 stood favourite. Write the name of the Urbs: PΩMH. Give the Greek value for each letter:

P
						=
						100
					
					
						Ω
						=
						800
					
					
						M
						=
						40
					
					
						H
						=
						8
					
				
				
					
						
						948
					
				
			
			

Deduct the Year of the Foundation of the City:

948
					
					
						753
					
				
				
					
						195
					
				
			
			

—the number of the Year of the Destruction of the City, and all else besides.

===Chinese===
有一阵子， 195 年最受青睐。写下永恒之城的名字：PΩMH。给每个字母赋以希腊数值：

P = 100
Ω = 800
M = 40
H = 8
（合计）　948

减去罗马城奠基之年的纪年：

948
−753
———
　195

——这就是罗马城覆灭之年的数目，其他一切也尽在其中。

===Original===
What proportion of the people held by this prophecy we have no worthy evidence. Certain it is that before that date and, when it turned out to be wrong, for more than eight centuries after it, all other numeral years, such as 365 the Year of Days, 6,300 the Grand Climacteric, and the giant numbers of First Chronicles Twenty-One, were swallowed up by the only figure specified in the New Testament itself: the *χίλια ἔτη* of Revelation Twenty. Other theories were brought into line. Christ’s “this generation” meant this millennium, this chiliad. The year One Thousand was The Year.

Christianity, still a persecuted creed offering no hope of present triumph, needed some such faith in not too distant punishment, and in not too distant reward and joy, to enable it to increase its strength and maintain its courage. For if what was down to happen at the Thousand spelt doom for the unbeliever, the prospects held out to the believer were correspondingly bright. The end of the world﻿—surely. But only as incidental to the Parousia, the Second Coming, the New Earth, and Christ’s millennial reign upon it great and glorious, which only His children would share. Millenarianism, seconding martyrdom, proved most successful of recruiting sergeants. The Christians grew rapidly.

Tertullian was a chiliast, and Commodian; also Cerinthus, Apollinaris, Julius Africanus, Nepos of Egypt, Methodius of Tyre. Many ardent sects, chiefly on the still uncertain borderline between Judaism and Christianity, Ebonists and Montanists and others, made chiliasm their main tenet. Soon, however, the opposition showed itself more powerful. The Alogi, the Gnostics, the neo-Platonist mystics were all anti-millennial and, after some early famous wavering, Saint Augustine. By this time the catastrophic atmosphere was clearing away. Bishops reconciled to the Empire found it impolitic, when not impossible, to consider its thousand years a thousand years of sin ending in fire and doom; to allow Rome Galilean no better portion than Rome Capitoline. Self-interest, re-christened commonsense, forbade them to think millennially. Only madmen, sorcerers, Asiatics, could believe in such Rabbinical vapouring. Only heretics; after Augustine chiliasm was proclaimed a heresy.

No longer necessary as a propagandist tenet, a bait to the sensual-minded, the millennial belief gradually changed in character. The optimistic element, the watchers for the glorious new kingdom on earth, dropped away; the pessimistic element, the waiters for the inglorious end of the old kingdom of earth, only remained. Chiliasm was left to the catastrophists; the Year would be the Year of Doom unqualified.

===Chinese===
这条预言曾笼络民众几何，我们没有像样的证据。可以确定的是：在那个日期之前，以及在它被证明落空之后的八个多世纪里，其余一切数字纪年——诸如 365 「日数之年」、 6,300 「大厄运之年」、《历代志上》第二十一章里的庞大数目——都被新约圣经本身唯一明载的数字吞没了：《启示录》第二十章的「χίλια ἔτη」（一千年）。别的理论都被拉来与之对齐。基督所说的「这一世代」，指的是这一个千年，这一个千禧之世。主后一千年，便是「那一年」。

当时的基督教仍是一个受迫害的信条，给人现世得胜的指望全无，正需要这样一种信心——相信惩罚不会太远，报偿与欢欣也不会太远——才能增长力量、保持勇气。因为，倘若千禧之年注定给不信者带来厄运，那么应许给信主之人的前景也就相应地光明。世界的终结——那是必然的。但它只是基督再临的附带之事：救主重临、新地降临、基督在其上伟大而荣耀地作王千年，唯有祂的儿女才能有份。千禧年主义辅佐着殉道精神，被证明是最有成效的募兵军曹。基督徒迅速增长。

德尔图良是千禧年论者，科莫迪安（Commodian）也是；此外还有赛林图斯（Cerinthus）、阿波里拿留（Apollinaris）、尤利乌斯·阿非利加努斯（Julius Africanus）、埃及的尼波斯（Nepos）、推罗的麦托丢（Methodius of Tyre）。许多狂热的教派，主要栖身于犹太教与基督教之间那条尚不明朗的边界线上——伊便尼派（Ebonists）、孟他努派（Montanists）等等——都把千禧年论奉为主要信条。然而不久，反对的声浪便显出更强的力量。阿罗基派（Alogi）、诺斯替派、新柏拉图主义的神秘主义者，统统反对千禧年说；圣奥古斯丁在早年那次著名的摇摆之后，也站到了这一边。到这时，大灾变的气氛正在消散。与帝国和解的主教们发现，把帝国的千年看作以烈火与末日收场的千年罪恶，即便不是绝无可能，也是不合时宜的；岂能容加利利的罗马，分得与卡比托利的罗马好不了多少的命运。私利改名为常识之后，便不许他们再作千禧年之想。只有疯子、巫师、亚洲人，才会相信这类拉比式的梦呓。只有异端——奥古斯丁之后，千禧年论被宣布为异端。

千禧年信仰既不再需要充当宣传的信条、充当引诱重感官者的香饵，其性质便逐渐改变了。乐观的成分——守望地上荣耀新国降临的人——脱落了；悲观的成分——等候地上旧国不光彩终结的人——唯独留存。千禧年论留给了灾变论者；那一年，将是不加任何限定的末日之年。

===Original===
The generations passed. The time drew near. The last century, the last year, the last hour.﻿ ﻿…

What happened?

What indeed? There are not four problems in all post-Classical history where views so flatly opposite are so flatly asserted and denied.

===Chinese===
一代又一代过去了。时候近了。最后一个世纪，最后一年，最后一个时辰。……

后来怎样？

怎样了？是啊。在整个后古典时代的历史里，凑不出四个像这样的问题，能让针锋相对的见解被这样不容分说地各执一端。

===Original===
The millennial terror was the one universal terror Europe has ever shared.﻿—It is a pure invention of later historians.

The whole world trembled and waited; a thing condemned to death, counting the mad hours.﻿—There’s no trace of those tremblings. A few stray zealots may, as in every later generation also, have harboured such notions. Even of these there is scant record.

The great men of the age announced it, such as Bernard of Thuringia.﻿—Great men such as: who else? And who, pray, was he?

There is a lease of 971 preserved; it is for twenty-nine years exactly.﻿—The usual period; there are leases of 981 and 990 for the same term.

*Appropinquante mundi termino* begin fearfully the wills of that time.﻿—A mere pious opening flourish, commoner moreover through the eighth and ninth centuries than in the tenth. Chronicles, as freer from stock-phraseology, bear surer witness than wills, and not all the rich chronicles of the period, from monastery by Rhine or Po or Danube, convey one slenderest hint.

===Chinese===
千禧年的恐怖，是欧洲有史以来唯一一次全民共有的恐怖。——它纯属后世史家的杜撰。

整个世界战栗着等待，像一名死囚，数着疯狂的时辰。——那些战栗毫无踪迹可寻。或许有几个零散的狂热分子怀有过这类念头，正如后世每一代也总有几个那样。就连这些人，留下的记录也寥寥无几。

当世的大人物们宣布过它，比如图林根的伯纳德（Bernard of Thuringia）。——这样的大人物，比如还有谁？再说，这位仁兄究竟是谁？

有一份 971 年的租约保存至今，期限恰好二十九年。——那是通行的年限； 981 年和 990 年的租约，租期也是一样。

那个时代的遗嘱，都以「世界末日将近」（Appropinquante mundi termino）之类的话战战兢兢地开篇。——那不过是虚敬的开场套语，况且在八、九世纪之间比在十世纪更常见。编年史比遗嘱更少套语，是更可靠的证人；而那个时代所有卷帙繁富的编年史，无论出自莱茵河、波河还是多瑙河畔的修道院，没有一部透露出哪怕最细微的一丝暗示。

===Original===
The Council of Trosly, in 909, declared the Thousand.﻿—The Council of Poitiers, in 999 (a more pressing, more impressive date), declared a few new punishments for bawdy clerks.

The Crusades were at rock-bottom but cringings, to acquire merit against the imminent end.﻿—Pope Sylvester, in his letter of 999, has no allusion.

Gifts of piety, the twin-brother of fear, the Donations were specially frequent in the last generation before the Thousand.﻿—They were specially infrequent.

The later nine-hundreds witnessed a frenzy of church-building, propitiatory and significant.﻿—This was but one symptom of the recovery of all the arts. Was then that glorious Palace of the Doge a palace of God? Could that new ceremony, wedding his city with the ring and high splendour as Bride of the Adriatic, have been reconciled with a belief that the Bridal Hour of God was for the morrow?

Of the signs of the *Dies Irae* not one was wanting. Wars from country to country, from castle to orgied castle. Feudal barons, feudal bandits, invading Christ’s churches to fill them with horrible songs; raiding holy nunneries to fling Christ’s sisters into dungeons, chained there for outrage; treading the common man Christ had come to save down everywhere under heel and sword. Christ’s Vicar himself on impious knees before Astarte. Everywhere robbing, sacking, murdering, suffering; highways troubled, forests filled with violence. Huns from over the Alps, Saracens from over the Straits, Vikings from over the north waters. Everywhere famine and pest: for food men hunted men, or stole into the churchyards and dug up dead men for their nourishing; of the black plague of the nine-nineties none died but with loathsome face and in pain more loathsome. Dragons in the sky, stars falling that were tears for man’s fate, rain of blood, rivers of blood. Church bells ringing out in the unseen night, tolled by unseen hands﻿—or by the beasts, as when in Orleans Cathedral a black wolf caught the rope between tooth and claw. Devils abroad in the perishing countryside, Antichrists rising up in every province, wolves storming the cities and witches the altars; cruelty and darkness over all lands.﻿—True, the hour was gloomy. But what hour of human history is not?

===Chinese===
特罗斯利会议（Trosly）于 909 年宣告了那千年。——普瓦捷会议于 999 年（一个更紧迫、更震撼的年份）宣告的，是对行淫的教士新增几项惩罚。

十字军东征说到底不过是卑躬屈膝的献媚，好为那迫近的终局积攒功德。——教皇西尔维斯特（Sylvester）在 999 年的书信里对此只字未提。

虔敬的馈赠是恐惧的孪生兄弟；在千年之前的最后一代人里，各类捐赠格外频繁。——那些捐赠格外稀少。

九百年代后期掀起了一股疯狂的建堂热潮，既是禳灾的，又是意味深长的。——那不过是百艺复苏的一个症状。难道那座辉煌的总督宫竟是上帝的宫殿？难道那套新仪式——总督以戒指和盛大排场将他的城娶为亚得里亚海的新娘——能与「上帝的婚期就在明日」的信仰相安无事？

《末日经》的种种征兆，一样也不曾缺少。国与国交战，城堡与纵乐的城堡相攻。封建贵族、封建强盗闯进基督的教堂，让里面充满可怕的歌声；袭击神圣的修女院，把基督的姊妹们抛进地牢，锁上锁链听凭蹂躏；把基督降世要拯救的普通人，到处踩在脚跟与刀剑之下。基督的代理人自己，正屈着亵渎的双膝向阿斯塔蒂（Astarte）下拜。处处是劫掠、洗劫、凶杀与苦难；大道不宁，林间充满暴行。匈人翻越阿尔卑斯山而来，萨拉森人渡过海峡而来，维京人横渡北方水域而来。遍地饥馑与瘟疫：人为粮食而猎取人，或者溜进教堂墓地，掘出死人以为滋养；九九〇年代的黑死病，死者无不面目可憎，死状更可憎。天上有龙，陨星如为人运命垂泪，血雨，血河。教堂的钟在无形的黑夜里鸣响，被无形的手敲动——或者被牲畜敲动，如奥尔良大教堂里那只黑狼，用牙和爪勾住钟绳。魔鬼出没于凋敝的乡野，敌基督在各行省纷纷立起，豺狼冲击城市，女巫冲击祭坛；残忍与黑暗笼罩诸邦。——不错，那个时辰是阴惨的。可是人类历史上哪个时辰不阴惨？

===Original===
The arguments balance without tilting. The belief was universal; it was nonexistent.

The facts tilted. The Year came; the end of the world came not.

Though discouraged, the chiliasts were not dismayed. There had been a slight miscalculation; it was not the year One Thousand, but the one thousandth year from Christ’s mission, Passion; and so 1030 and 1033 offered new chances. These proved not less illusory. The millennial fear lay down beside the millennial hope, buried together.

===Chinese===
天平两端的论证不相上下。这信仰无所不在；它子虚乌有。

事实让天平倾斜了。那一年来了；世界末日没有来。

千禧年论者虽然受了挫，却没有丧胆。那不过是一次小小的失算：不是主后一千年，而是基督传道与受难之后的第一千年；于是 1030 年和 1033 年又提供了新的机会。这些机会被证明同样虚妄。千禧年的恐惧在千禧年的希望身旁躺下，双双合葬。

===Original===
Disaster of avoided disaster shook the Church awhile. Magic raised its apostate head. Witches’ sabbaths rivalled holy Sundays; off minster towers the gargoyles elbowed the angels. Name ousted Number, Antichrist not End governed recovering religion’s next centuries of expectation.

The close of the Middle Ages saw a revival. Pseudo-Methodius, rediscovered, proved a rich date-mine; also the Sibylline Leaves. St. Vincent Ferrier counted the verses in the Psalms and plumped for 2537, yet to test. 1496, a year with much sound astrological backing, opened auspiciously when they fished out of the Tiber a monster with donkey’s head, maiden’s body, stag’s right foot, griffin’s left, and old grey face instead of rump﻿—but ended like every other year, without the end. Stoffler chose February of 1524, and by flood, as Saturn, Mars and Jupiter would then be together in the Fishes. Doctor Auriol with his ark made ready; it was the driest February known. Stoffler tried again. Hostile contemporaries, with a sneer, note 1588 as unusually barren of happenings; surely an exaggeration, for the waters covered, if not the earth, at least the Great Armada.

In the Roman Church itself, numerical prophecy has had slightest renewal; it was into the hands of the Rabbis and the Protestants, witches of Endor and warlocks of England, that the game now passed. The Fifth Monarchy Men excelled in it, and cruel Cromwell and cynical Charles excelled each other in harrying them. The Rabbis revelled in one pseudo-Messianic year after another: apostates like Sabbatai Zevi, Sabbataic quacks like Mordecai of Eisenstadt, and sincere and frantic visionaries unlike either, proclaimed Antichrist born in Babylon almost every year, and each was to be the last. Even 1666, the Beast’s, the Neronic, though with London in flames it stood not signless, ended tamely; by merely ending.

===Chinese===
躲过的灾难反过来成了灾难，令教会震颤了一阵。魔法抬起了它叛教的头。女巫的安息日与神圣的主日分庭抗礼；大教堂塔楼上的滴水兽把天使挤到了一边。名号驱逐了数目；在复兴中的宗教其后几个世纪的期待里，施行统治的是敌基督，而不是终结。

中世纪临近结束时又见一次复兴。被重新发掘的伪麦托丢（Pseudo-Methodius）证明是一座丰富的日期矿藏；西卜林残卷亦然。圣文森特·费雷尔（St. Vincent Ferrier）数了数《诗篇》的诗节，敲定 2537 年，尚待检验。 1496 年是有充分星象学依据的一年，开局吉祥——人们从台伯河里捞起一头怪物，驴的头、少女的身、雄鹿的右足、狮鹫的左足，本该是屁股的地方换成一张灰白的老脸——但它和每一个别的年份一样收了场，没有末日。施托夫勒（Stoffler）选定了 1524 年 2 月，以洪水灭世，因为土星、火星和木星届时将同聚双鱼宫。奥里奥尔博士（Doctor Auriol）备好了方舟；那是已知最干旱的一个二月。施托夫勒再试了一次。心怀敌意的同代人不无讥讽地记道， 1588 年格外无事可记；这想必是夸大之词，因为那年的洪水纵未覆盖大地，至少覆盖了无敌舰队。

在罗马教会自身，数字预言几乎未曾复兴；这场游戏如今转到了拉比们和新教徒们、隐多珥的女巫们与英格兰的男巫们手里。第五王国派（Fifth Monarchy Men）于此道最为擅长，而残酷的克伦威尔与玩世的查理在迫害他们上又各自争先。拉比们沉醉于一个又一个伪弥赛亚之年：像沙巴泰·泽维（Sabbatai Zevi）那样的叛教者、像艾森施塔特的末底改（Mordecai of Eisenstadt）那样的沙巴泰式庸医，以及与两者都不同、既真诚又狂乱的幻视者，几乎年年宣称敌基督已生于巴比伦，而每一年都该是最后一年。就连 1666 年——兽的年份、尼禄的年份——虽然伦敦正陷于烈火、征象不可谓无，也了结得温温吞吞；不过是随年岁终了而终了。

===Original===
The nineteenth century revival was relative only; the fact that such beliefs were making headway again had no more effect on the century’s triumphant march than its belief in Christianity itself.

In England the number of folk so convinced of this date or that as on the whole to order their lives in accordance with their conviction may now and then have reached seven figures; the number who went further, and in view of the worldly end sold all their worldly goods, can rarely have reached two. If the quality of the enthusiasts must, by the ordinary snobbery of social standards or the extraordinary snobbery of intellectual ones, be put lower than the quantity, that matters little: most of what Science teaches is as silly, and much of what dukes believe. If their enthusiasm was sorely tried, that mattered less; failure never deterred the prophets from beginning over again, and new prophets from beginning afresh, and old ones from being disinterred.

Mother Shipton, for instance, was dragged in splendour from her merited obscurity. Our school stood near her cave, but we entered it rarely, as the revival of her reputation had raised the admission-fee to the (for some of us) unmanageable sum of threepence. In the eighties she did good work:

===Chinese===
十九世纪的复兴只是相对而言；这类信念重又抬头的事实，对这个世纪凯歌行进的影响，并不比这个世纪自身对基督教的信仰更大。

在英格兰，因笃信这个或那个日期、并大体据此安排一生的人数，或许偶尔上过七位数；至于更进一步、因俗世的将终而变卖了全部家产的人数，则难得达到两人。倘若按社会标准的寻常势利、或知识标准的非凡势利，这些热心者的质地必得被估得比人数更低，那也无伤大雅：科学所教导的大部分东西同样愚蠢，公爵们所信的许多东西亦然。至于他们的热心饱受挫折，那就更无关紧要了；失败从不曾阻止先知们从头再来，新的先知重新起头，旧的先知被人重新发掘出来。

例如希普顿婆婆（Mother Shipton），就被风光地从她应得的湮没无闻里拖了出来。我们的学校就坐落在她的岩洞附近，但我们难得进去一回，因为她名声的复兴已把门票抬到了三便士——这对我们中间的一些人是无力负担的巨款。一八八〇年代，她干了一件好事：

===Original===
The world to an end will come
					

					In eighteen hundred and eighty-one.

Spring passed, the fatal season, spring in which earth was created and, as most think, shall be decreated; and autumn, and the last of December. Through half England, in north and west, in the three widest shires most widely, villagers had spent whole nights in the fields confessing their sins to the dark skies and crying for mercy. Crying wasted: for nothing happened.

Ah! ’twas a trifling error, a minor misreading of the text, reliance on a corrupted version. The true version ran:

===Chinese===
世界末日必将应验，

　　就在一八八一年。

春天过去了——那是致命的季节：大地受造于春，而且如多数人所想，也将被反造于春；秋天过去了，十二月的最末一天也过去了。在半个英格兰，在北方和西方，在最辽阔的三个郡里最甚，村民们整夜整夜守在田野里，向黑暗的天空忏悔自己的罪，哭求怜悯。哭也无益：因为什么也没有发生。

啊！那是个小小的失误，对文本的一处小小误读，错信了讹误的版本。正确的版本是这样的：

===Original===
The world at an end we’ll view
					

					In eighteen hundred and eighty-two.

This was a falling off. There was a falling off also in the night attendance in the fields, so discouraging indeed that Mother Shipton was put to grave again, and none went to the trouble of inventing

===Chinese===
世界完蛋我们眼见，

　　就在一八八二年。

这是一次退步。夜里到田野守夜的人数也有了退步，着实令人气馁，于是希普顿婆婆被重新送进了坟墓，再没有人肯费心去发明

===Original===
—at an end we’ll see,
					

					—at an end, be sure!
					

					—at an end will arrive﻿ ﻿…

as hopeful variorum readings.

On the European Continent, even in Germany and disastrous Russia, such fears have counted for less; in the United States of America for much more, where great sects have arisen, like those in the Early Church, with the millennial hope and fear as their main source of inspiration, recruitment and income. One year the fear became almost nationwide. Its protagonist was William Miller.

===Chinese===
——完蛋时我们将亲见，

——完蛋，错不了！

——完蛋终将到来……

之类可供寄望的集校异文。

在欧洲大陆，即便在德国和灾难深重的俄国，这类恐慌也算不得什么；在美利坚合众国则算得重大得多——那里兴起过一些大教派，一如早期教会中的那些，以千禧年的希望与恐惧为灵感、募众和进账的主要来源。有一年，这恐惧几乎席卷全国。它的主角是威廉·米勒（William Miller）。

===Original===
---

William Miller was a simple New Englander. He had served against Britain in the Canadian War, and after that one experience of the wider world settled down to farm life in the country. There he got religion, and religion him. He became a student of the Prophets, especially of Daniel, in whose apocalyptic pages God suddenly showed him the Secret: in the year 1843 the world would stop.

William had no fear for himself; only fear that the time might be too short for him to spread the tidings to those who were chosen, and for them to prepare their souls for the day of destruction they alone would escape. He seems to have been a disinterested man, desiring neither power, admiration nor money. It was the activity of certain rivals which spurred him to action﻿—Harriet Livermore was proclaiming 1847; Joseph Wolff, of Jerusalem, was waiting for the end in Jerusalem; Captain Saunders, of Liverpool, was waiting for it in Liverpool. William asked and obtained leave from heaven, and publicly announced his revelation to some friends:

===Chinese===
---

威廉·米勒是个朴实的新英格兰人。他在加拿大战争中与英国人打过仗，那一回见过世面之后，便回乡务农定居。在乡下他得了宗教，宗教也得了他。他成了众先知——尤其是但以理——的学生；就在那些启示录式的篇章里，上帝突然向他显明了那个秘密： 1843 年，世界将会停摆。

威廉对自己并不忧惧；他只怕时间太短，来不及把消息传给那些蒙拣选的人，来不及让他们为自己独能幸免的那毁灭之日预备灵魂。他似乎是个无所私图的人，不贪权势，不求赞叹，不要钱财。倒是某些同行的活跃催他行动起来——哈丽特·利弗莫尔（Harriet Livermore）正在宣讲 1847 年；耶路撒冷的约瑟夫·沃尔夫（Joseph Wolff）正在耶路撒冷等待终结；利物浦的桑德斯船长（Captain Saunders）正在利物浦等它。威廉向上天请示并蒙了批准，随后向几位朋友公开宣布了他的启示：

===Original===
Calculation I
				
					
						From the date of the commandment to rebuild Jerusalem, BC 457, to the Crucifixion of Christ: 70 weeks. A “week” equals 7 years
						
						490
					
					
						From the Crucifixion of Christ to “taking away the daily Abomination,” or Paganism
						
						475
					
					
						From the taking away of Pagan rites to the setting up of the Abomination of Desolation (Papal Civil Rule)
						
						30
					
					
						From the setting up of the Papal Abomination to the end thereof
						
						1260
					
					
						From the taking away of Papal Civil Rule to the first resurrection and the end of the World in 1843
						
						45
					
				
				
					
						
						2300
					
				
			
			

The clue number was thus 2300. Proceeding:

===Chinese===
计算一
从重建耶路撒冷的命令之日（公元前 457 ）到基督被钉十字架： 70 个「七」；一「七」等于 7 年　　490
从基督被钉十字架到「除掉常献的憎恶」，即异教　　475
从异教礼仪被除掉到「设立那毁坏可憎的」（教皇的世俗统治）　　30
从教皇那憎恶的设立到它的终结　　1260
从教皇世俗统治的除掉到头一次复活及 1843 年世界之终　　45
　　　　（合计）　2300

如此，线索数字便是 2300 。继续算：

===Original===
Calculation II
				
					
						From
						
						2300
					
					
						Subtract 70 “weeks”
						
						490
					
					
						
						1810
					
					
						Add to this the term of the Saviour’s life
						
						33
					
				
				
					
						End of the World in
						
						1843
					
				
			
			

The proof was irrefragable.

But God in His Word had heaped up proof upon proof:

						475

						1843

						2300

							BC
						
						457
					
				
				
					
						End of the World in
						
							AD
						
						1843

							BC

计算二
从　2300
减去 70 个「七」　490
余　1810
再加上救主在世的寿数　33
世界终于　1843

这证明无可辩驳。

但上帝在他的圣言里，已把证明堆上加证明：

计算三
从基督被钉十字架到除掉常献的憎恶（第一算之项）　475
加上救主的年龄 33 与但以理的数目 1335　1368

计算四

计算五
以色列家还须因罪受罚「七次」（《利未记》第二十六章），即七个 360 日之年，等于　2520
减去初次被掳往巴比伦之年　公元前 677
　　　　**世界之终**在　1843

===Original===
And so on.

The exact day, though based on proofs less profuse, was also revealed; it would be the Vernal Equinox. No more world by midnight on March 21st, 1843.

Faith, hope and self-charity combined to win for the prediction an immediate and all-absorbing place in hundreds of souls. Within a few months the Millerites had become a sect, spreading rapidly throughout the Bay State and beyond its borders. Wild camp-meetings were held at night, when fervency of expectation inspired to Pentecostal utterance and unutterable joy.

===Chinese===
诸如此类。

确切的日子虽然所据证明不那么浩繁，也同样启示了出来：那将是春分。到 1843 年 3 月 21 日午夜，世界便不复存在。

信心、盼望与自爱协力，为这条预言在成百颗灵魂里赢得了立竿见影、吞噬一切的位置。不出几个月，米勒派（Millerites）已成一个教派，迅速传遍海湾州及其境外。夜间举行狂野的营地布道会，期待的炽热激发出五旬节式的言语和不可言传的喜乐。

===Original===
If there were believers, there were also exploiters. William Miller was a poor stick﻿—so thought Elder Joshua V. Hines﻿—a humble *illuminé* quite unfit to look after the business side, and the brass-band side, of what was already a mighty Movement. Joshua would do that. Joshua did. A big organized campaign was launched: “End of the World for ’43.” The combination of prophet and salesman proved a fruitful one; the Millerites waxed. Nor need the Elder’s complex soul detain us. Hypocrites are fewer than alleged, and deceivers most often self-deceivers; the movement was a splendid one to be in; Joshua derived pathological delight from its excitements, delicious pride from its leadership and (it was whispered) ample booty from the openhandedness of its adherents. Besides, it might all be true.

If there were exploiters, there were also enemies. Orthodox pulpits denounced these pernicious doctrines, so harmful to their own. More malignant tongues averred that the Millerite tabernacle would not be ready for dedication till the May of ’43, some weeks after the Equinox; it was replied that only the year was a revelation, there being a “margin of error” for the actual day. Really nasty folk alleged that the building was insured for seven years; this was denied, and to clinch their sincerity the Millerites pointed to the furniture they had given away, the farms they had sold, the orchards they had cut down, the crops they were abandoning.

Then came the Comet. The most terrible and triumphant of modern times, three hundred million miles long, a svelte sword straight and unwavering, cutting the night-sky in two. The Sign! William was faint with ecstasy. New believers flocked forward; unbelievers wavered. The 21st dawned. Last preparations had been made. Before evening armies of men and women were seen moving out from Boston into the open country and up on to hillocks, whence heaven could receive them most conveniently. Thousands more followed to watch them, and maybe also﻿ ﻿… The sun dropped; the stars came out. As earth’s last darkness fell, some shivered; then joined the others in shouting Hallelujah till midnight and the trump of doom. Pandemonium and paroxysm reigned.

===Chinese===
有信的人，也就有借信牟利的人。威廉·米勒是根不中用的木头——约书亚·V·海因斯长老（Elder Joshua V. Hines）如此想——一个谦卑的「受启者」（illuminé），全然不配打理这场已然声势浩大的运动的事务面，和铜管乐队面。这些自有约书亚来。约书亚也果真做了。一场组织庞大的宣传战就此发动：「 43 年，世界末日」。先知加推销员的组合被证明多产；米勒派壮大了。至于这位长老复杂的灵魂，无需在此多费笔墨。伪君子比传闻的少，骗人者多半先骗了自己；这运动身在其中实在体面；约书亚从它的兴奋里获得病态的欢愉，从它的领袖地位获得美妙的骄傲，还（有人嘀咕）从信众的慷慨解囊获得丰厚的进项。况且，说不定这一切都是真的。

有借信牟利的人，也就有敌人。正统的讲坛痛斥这些有害的教义——对它们自家的教义为害甚烈。更恶毒的舌头则断言，米勒派的会堂要到 43 年五月才能落成奉献，比春分晚了好几个星期；得到的答复是：启示只关乎年份，具体的日子存在「误差幅度」。真正刻薄的人扬言那座建筑保了七年的险；这一条遭到了否认，而为了坐实自己的诚恳，米勒派指给人看他们送掉的家具、卖掉的田庄、砍倒的果园、正弃置的庄稼。

接着，彗星来了。那是近代最可怖、最得胜的一颗，长三亿英里，一柄修长笔直而毫不偏斜的剑，把夜空剖成两半。那征兆！威廉喜得几乎晕去。新的信者蜂拥而来；不信的人动摇了。二十一日破晓。最后的准备都已就绪。傍晚之前，只见一队队男女撤出波士顿，走进旷野，登上小丘——从那里上天迎接他们最为方便。还有数千人跟去看他们，或许也是为了……太阳落了；星星出来了。大地最后的黑暗降临时，有些人打了个寒噤；随后便加入众人，高呼哈利路亚，直呼到午夜与末日的号角。嚣乱与痉挛君临一切。

===Original===
The hour came.

And went.

Nothing happened. Nothing. In misery of beaten hope, through the dawn of March 22nd the crowds moved homewards crushed and silent, or weeping.

In another place a group waited in white ascension-robes; a suggestion of Elder Joshua’s, though when these wedding garments proved ineffective he promptly denied it. One man fitted a turkey’s wings to his shoulders, and tried to fly; he fell and broke his arm. At Westford, Mass., the local Millerites had assembled in their farmhouse headquarters, proposing to spend earth’s ultimate evening together in prayer and praise until, just before the midnight hour, they would move out into the open for their ascension. Now Crazy Amos, the local drunk, was not a believer. Knowing the Millerites were all safe indoors, he stole out on to the village green hard by, and blew a great blast on a great horn that was his cherished toy. The listeners heard, in a body rushed out, madly jostled each other as they fought for the best places to be caught up from. Standing aside in the discreet darkness, Crazy Amos put his horn to his mouth and blew a still more fearful blast, and another, and another. Glory! Glory! Hallelujah! they shouted, raising their arms with almost unbearable yearning to heaven. A few actually rose in the air, the Second Ascension had literally begun, when others, whom faith had not prevailed to carry off their feet, spied Amos blaring away. The reaction of shame, disappointment and fatigue was terrible. Like a flock of scared sheep they shambled indoors, Crazy Amos jeering after them: “Fools! go dig your potatoes! For the Angel Gabriel he won’t go a-digging ’em for you.”

===Chinese===
时候到了。

又过去了。

什么也没有发生。什么也没有。怀着希望被击碎的惨痛，人群穿过 3 月 22 日的黎明，颓然无声地走回家去，有的一路哭泣。

在另一处，有一群人穿着白色升天袍等候；那是约书亚长老的提议，虽然这些婚礼礼服被证明无效之后，他立刻矢口否认。有个人把火鸡的翅膀绑在肩上，试着飞翔；他摔下来，断了胳膊。在马萨诸塞州韦斯特福德（Westford, Mass.），当地的米勒派聚在他们的农舍总部里，打算一同用祷告和赞美度过尘世的最后一晚，直到午夜将至，再走到露天升天。本地醉汉「疯子埃莫斯」（Crazy Amos）并不是信徒。他知道米勒派都安然待在屋里，便溜到近旁的村中草地上，吹响了他珍爱的玩具——一支大号角。屋里的人一听，全体冲了出来，疯了一样推搡拥挤，抢占有利的升天之位。疯子埃莫斯站在稳妥的暗处旁观，把号角举到嘴边，吹出更加骇人的一声，接着又是一声，又一声。荣耀！荣耀！哈利路亚！他们呼喊着，怀着几乎难忍的渴望向天举臂。有几个人真的腾空而起，第二次升天已然实实在在地开始，这时，那些信心还不足以双脚离地的人，认出了正在吹个不停的埃莫斯。羞惭、失望与疲惫一齐反扑，其势可怕。他们像一群受惊的羊，拖着脚步蹭回屋里，疯子埃莫斯在背后讥笑他们：「傻瓜们！回去挖你们的土豆吧！天使加百列可不会替你们去挖。」

===Original===
So everywhere the night had come and gone; the prophecy was a delusion, William and Joshua deceivers.

But saints, like scientists, have rich subterfuge in modification that laymen must envy them, and a hold on their followers that most naked defeat does not lightly relax. Some of these did indeed drop away, for whom the emotional recoil was too violent, or the rage at finding themselves without their chattels. For the most part they held fast, gobbling up with a greed of sincerity equalling William’s own the new subtleties﻿—and new dates﻿—he was discovering. The Year was all he had ever announced as part of the revelation proper; that gave them nine months yet, till the last day of December. Ill in body and soul, he started a still more intensive study of the Prophet. At once he perceived his mistake, his foolish impossible mistake! The year of course was the *Jewish* year, so the end was for the spring equinox of forty-*four*. The faithful grasped gladly at these straws, and new crowds, diminished in size but not certainty, went forth to the hills again when March again came round.

Nothing happened. Nothing. But only when the last outside hope﻿—December 31st, 1844, the last day of the Christian year in which the Jewish year ended﻿—proved also vain, when the ultimate twists and tricks of re-modification and reinterpretation had been exposed and exploded by inexorable fact, did the movement peter finally out. It was the end also of old William Miller, who died brokenhearted; but not of the world.

===Chinese===
各处都是如此：夜来了又去了；预言是一场骗局，威廉和约书亚是骗子。

然而圣徒们和科学家们一样，自有外行人不能不艳羡的丰富遁词——即所谓修正——而且对追随者有一种掌控，多数赤裸裸的失败也不易松脱。信众中确有一些人退出了，他们或因情绪的反冲过于剧烈，或因发现自己囊中一空而狂怒。但大多数人坚持了下来，以一种不亚于威廉本人的赤诚之贪，狼吞虎咽地消化着他正不断发现的新玄机——和新日期。作为启示本体的部分，他历来只宣布过那一年；这就还给他们留出九个月，直到十二月的最后一天。他身心俱病，却开始更加发奋地研读那位先知。他立刻看出了自己的错误，他那愚蠢得不可思议的错误！那一年当然是*犹太*年，所以终结当在四*四*年的春分。信徒们欣然抓住这些稻草；到了来年三月，新的人群——规模缩小了，笃信没有缩小——又一次走向群山。

什么也没有发生。什么也没有。然而直到最后一线外围希望—— 1844 年 12 月 31 日，犹太年结束于其内的那个基督徒年份的最后一天——也被证明落空，直到重新修正与重新解释的一切终极曲笔与花招都被无情的事实戳穿、爆破，这场运动才终于渐渐消散。这也是老威廉·米勒的终结，他伤心而死；但不是世界的终结。

===Original===
---

No latter year of fate has had quite such a following.
